DEC-587 | ot2e, first task (DEC-537: not verified by the UX pass): how does a rewatch run behave through sync clients, the offline queue stamps (DEC-455) and the importers, and what marks a run? | Trace, read from the code on 2026-11-11. (1) Today's rule (`app/src/library.rs::log_progress_stamped`): only `finished` moves a status to Completed and only Planned moves to In progress; the move is stored on the event as `status_change` and applied with LWW on the stamp (the server's, or the client's `hlc` of a queued log, DEC-455); an episode logged on a Completed entry carries no move. No event today carries Completed -> In progress or, on an episode, -> Completed, so both are free to mean "run". (2) The plan's marker (ux-owner-test.md 1.4: a flag `rewatch_run` on the log call; the first rewatched episode carries Completed -> In progress) does not cover the menu's "Start a rewatch": that start has no episode to carry the move, and a status set by hand writes no event. Without one more stored bit a run with no episode yet ("Rewatch: 0 of 24", next step S1 E1) cannot be told from a show that is up to date and In progress. (3) Choice: the start is its own event, `Progress::Rewatch` (`{"type":"rewatch"}`, episodic model only, payload of the existing jsonb column: no migration, no new column). Logged on a Completed entry it carries `status_change` Completed -> In progress; on any other status it carries none and starts nothing. Derived from the effective history in its order (`occurred_at`, id): the run = the newest start that carries the move, its episodes = the regular episodes logged after it; it is finished when one of them carries -> Completed (`log_progress_stamped` writes that move on the event that makes the run cover every known regular episode, only while the entry is In progress), running while unfinished and the entry is In progress, stopped while unfinished and the entry is Completed (stop = the existing status write; no new call). Every episode logged while a run is running belongs to it, so no per-event flag and no `rewatch_run` field on `NewProgressDto`. (4) Sync clients: nothing changes for a client that does not know the type: its episode logs on a Completed entry stay extra views; it sees the start in the history like it sees `undo`; the status moves reach it as `library_entry` changes. A client starts a run with the existing `LogProgress` command; no new command type. (5) Offline queue (DEC-455): the start is queued as a log with the queue's stamp, the episodes after it in queue order; a replay is a no-op (op ids). The moves are LWW like every log's move: a status set later on another device wins. Then the start's move is on the event but not on the entry; with a later Completed that reads as a stopped run, with a later Paused or Dropped the run does not show (and no episode finishes it), with a later In progress it runs. A start that reaches an entry which is no longer Completed carries no move: its episodes are extra views. A stop queued after the start has the later stamp and wins. (6) Undo (DEC-208): undoing the start gives Completed back while the entry still carries that write, and the run is gone from the effective history; undoing the finishing episode reopens the run. (7) Importers: `importers/src/runner.rs` creates the entry with the file's status, logs the history through `log_progress` and sets the file's status last. Trakt and legacy rows never contain a start, so an import never starts a run and none of them changes (`crates/importers` untouched). Imported views are dated in the past: logged while a run is running they sort before its start and are not counted in it (membership is by history order, not by recording time), so an import cannot finish a run. The legacy "In progress with earlier finishes" entries (DEC-354) stay what they are today ("Up to date" or plain progress); they get no start because the file does not say when the rewatch began. The generic export and import carry the start like any other event (`history[].progress`). (8) Not a run: a movie (the single model refuses the type; a second `finished` on Completed moves nothing, as before); "Again" on one episode outside a run; a start on a show that is In progress (also an up-to-date one: the app cannot give Completed back at the end of what was never Completed; ux-owner-test.md 1.2 rule 6 offered the start there too). (9) Side effects accepted: the start is a row in the history and the activity lists and counts as one logged event in the stats (`events`); the episode that finishes a run counts as a completion in the stats and goals (`status_change ->> 'to' = 'completed'`), a stopped run does not. No SQL changes. | (a) the plan's flag on the first episode only, the menu arming the page until that episode (the armed state lives in one browser tab, the pill stays Completed after "Start"); (b) a column on `library_entries` (`rewatch_since`: a migration, a field in the sync entry and its own LWW rule; membership by time, so an import during a run would count); (c) a sixth status "Rewatching" (rejected in ux-owner-test.md 1.5: touches the approved status set, filters, stats, importers); (d) deriving a run from "In progress with a whole pass before the status stamp" (a status set back by hand for a new season would read as a rewatch) | remove `Progress::Rewatch` and the two moves in `log_progress_stamped`; stored starts then fail to parse, so first delete them (`DELETE FROM progress_events WHERE progress ->> 'type' = 'rewatch'`) or keep the variant unused | DEC-537; DEC-538; DEC-455; DEC-208; DEC-354; docs/fable/round2/ux-owner-test.md 1.2, 1.4; docs/fable/round2/next-packages.md ot2e; crates/app/src/library.rs; crates/api/src/sync.rs; crates/web/src/offline.rs; crates/importers/src/runner.rs, trakt.rs, legacy.rs
DEC-588 | ot2e: the Undo of "Log N again" takes a whole rewatch run back (its episodes newest first, then its start). The undo of the finishing episode restores In progress with a new stamp, so the undo of the start no longer found "exactly its write" on the entry (DEC-208) and left the show In progress. How does an undo recognise a write that another undo restored? | Two additions to the stored move, no migration (the `status_change` jsonb): (1) every `StatusChange` records `from_stamp`, the stamp of the write it replaced (absent on older events); (2) an undo stores the move it caused on its own event (`from` = what it took back, `to` = what it restored, its new stamp), like every other event does. `StatusChange::carried(entry stamp, write, history)`: the entry carries a write when its stamp is that write's, or when its stamp was written by an undo whose target had replaced that write (`from_stamp`), through any number of such undos. `App::undo_progress` uses it (`status_to_restore`). A status chosen by hand in between still stays: it has a stamp no undo wrote. Undo events are not part of the effective history, so stats, goals and the run derivation do not read their move. Test `app/tests/rewatch.rs::undoing_a_whole_run_gives_completed_back` (red: `in_progress` instead of `completed`; then green, with the hand-set case in the same test) | restore the old stamp with the old status (breaks LWW: clients that saw the newer stamp keep the newer value); let the start's undo set Completed whenever the entry is In progress (overwrites a status chosen by hand); undo the start first in the toast (the status would end In progress -> Completed -> ... in the wrong order and the finishing episode's undo would then find nothing to restore) | drop `from_stamp`, write `status_change: None` on undo events and compare `entry.status.stamp == c.stamp` in `app/src/library.rs` as before | DEC-208; DEC-587; crates/domain/src/progress.rs; crates/app/src/library.rs
DEC-589 | ot2e: choices made while building the rewatch run (model A, DEC-537; marker and rules in DEC-587) | (1) Where it starts: the overflow menu of a Completed show has "Start a rewatch" first (podcast: "Start listening again"); toast "Rewatch started" with Undo. The answer "Log N again" of the bulk question starts a run when the entry is Completed: the start, then the episodes, all dated now whatever the date choice says (review OT2A-10); on any other status the answer logs extra views as in ot2a. (2) While it runs: pill In progress; the line "Rewatch: 5 of 24 episodes. Watched once before."; the next step, the progress words and the bar on cards count within the run; the Episodes tab shows the run (what it reached is watched, the rest reads "Watched"/"Mark" again) and hides the date choice of the bulk marks (a backfilled view sorts before the start and would not count). The next step is the episode after the highest of the run; when that was the last known one, the first episode the run skipped. (3) Stop: the menu item for Completed reads "Stop the rewatch" while a run runs (one item, the same status write; the status select stops it too); the toast is the usual "Marked as Completed". Afterwards "Watched once; rewatch stopped after 5 of 24 episodes"; a run stopped without an episode says nothing. (4) Finish: the episode that makes the run cover every known regular episode sets Completed; its toast's second line is "Completed again: <title>" (the wording of ux-owner-test.md 1.3, "Rewatch finished. Watched 2 times.", was not taken: the toast does not know the count, the header shows it after the refetch). `ProgressEventDto.status` (additive) names the status an event moved the entry to. (5) The count (`app::times`): a run counts once. For a show it is the larger of the whole passes over the known episodes and "1 + runs that are over" (a run starts on a Completed entry, so there was a time before it; this also counts right for a show completed by hand with part of it logged). (6) Review OT2A-7: `ItemPageDto.times_at` is the day of the pass the count names (last finish; last play; for a show the day its last whole pass was done or its last run finished), and the count line uses it instead of the newest view; a completion by hand has no day ("Watched once"). (7) Not offered: a run on a show that is not Completed (DEC-587 (8)). (8) Wording per verb only where it must differ: podcasts get "listening again" for the menu, the start toast and the two lines; the finish toast and the history row ("Started again from the beginning") are neutral. Known limits, not built: a run that is running when the library is exported comes back without its start taking effect on re-import (the row is In progress when the start is replayed; the views are all there); the question of the bulk mark still says "as a rewatch" where the answer logs extra views (In progress shows); two logs at the same moment could both miss finishing a run (the pages then treat a run that covers every episode as over, and the status stays In progress until the person sets it) | start only from the bulk question (no way to start before the first episode); keep the full watched state in the list while a run runs (the list could not say where the rewatch is); a separate "stop" call and toast | per item: remove the menu item in `web/src/item.rs::overflow`; `again_starts_run` returns false; `season_rows_in` ignores the start; `fact_line` without the two run branches; `times` without `run_passes`; `fact_line` back to `summary.last_at` | DEC-537; DEC-538; DEC-564 (OT2A-7, OT2A-10); DEC-587; docs/fable/round2/ux-owner-test.md 1.2, 1.3; tests in crates/app/tests/rewatch.rs, crates/web/src/item_words.rs, episode_plan.rs, next_step.rs, apps/server/tests/it/item_page.rs, offline.rs, src/web/tests/item.spec.ts
DEC-590 | Review ot2e, OT2E-2 (must): a stop left no trace, so a stopped rewatch run came back with a later In progress and kept collecting views. What ends a run? | Orchestrator decision (the owner may reverse it): option (1) of the review. Stopping writes a marker, `Progress::RewatchStop` (`{"type": "rewatch_stop"}`, episodic model only, a payload of the existing jsonb column like the start: no migration, no new column). Logged while a run is open (its newest start has neither a finishing episode nor a stop marker) it carries In progress -> Completed like the finishing episode does (LWW on the stamp, DEC-455), and the run closes there: `domain::rewatch::runs` puts no later episode into it, `Run::phase` answers Stopped whatever the status says, so a later In progress by hand (a new season) shows no run, and views after the stop are plain extra views. Without an open run the stop is refused (`conflict`) and leaves no row. Undo of the stop reopens the run (DEC-208, DEC-588). The web's "Stop the rewatch" logs the marker (toast "Rewatch stopped" with Undo; offline it is queued as a log); the status select and the bulk status still write the plain status. A status write without the marker (an older client, the select) keeps the behaviour of DEC-587: the run reads as stopped while the entry is Completed, runs again with In progress, and collects later episodes until the next start or stop marker; this is kept on purpose and pinned by `a_run_stops_and_another_one_starts` (domain) and `undoing_a_stop_reopens_the_run` (app). The stop is one more event in `events` of the stats, like the start, and is not a completion: the three SQL statements that count `status_change ->> 'to' = 'completed'` leave `rewatch_stop` out (`store-pg/src/repos/library.rs`, `goals_groups.rs`, `stats_cache.rs`; `.sqlx` regenerated) and so does `app/src/stats.rs`. This replaces the wording of DEC-587: (3) "stopped while unfinished and the entry is Completed (stop = the existing status write; no new call)" now holds for a status write without the marker only; (4) and (8) "episode logs on a Completed entry stay extra views" hold after a stop marker and before any run, not after a plain status write; (5) a stop queued after the start is the marker. docs/modules/ROOT/pages/sync.adoc, section "Rewatch runs", says the same. Export and import: the markers are in the file, two stopped runs stay two (DEC-593) | option (2) of the review: a run is running only while the entry still carries the start's status write, any status chosen by hand ends it (no new data, but no pause and resume of a run, and views after a stop and the export stay wrong); a flag on the start type (`{"type": "rewatch", "end": true}`: one variant, but every match on the start would have to look at the flag); a status write that also appends the marker on the server (the plain select would then end a run for good and "pause" by status would be gone) | remove `Progress::RewatchStop`, `Run.stopped` and the stop item of `web/src/item.rs::RunMenu`; delete stored markers first (`DELETE FROM progress_events WHERE progress ->> 'type' = 'rewatch_stop'`), else they fail to parse | docs/reviews/2026-11-11-ot2e-review.md OT2E-2; DEC-587; DEC-589; tests domain `rewatch::tests::{a_stopped_run_does_not_come_back_with_in_progress, views_after_a_stop_do_not_join_the_run, two_stopped_runs_stay_apart, undo_takes_a_start_a_finish_and_a_stop_back}`, app `tests/rewatch.rs::{a_stop_marker_ends_the_run_for_good, undoing_a_stop_reopens_the_run}`, store-pg `phase1::stats_on_postgres_match_the_in_memory_rules`, server `it` `offline::a_rewatch_run_started_offline_replays_once`, `item_page::a_rewatch_run_shows_in_the_header_the_next_step_and_the_list`, `item.spec.ts` (all seen red first except the two `it` tests and the browser test, which were changed with the code)
DEC-591 | Review ot2e, OT2E-1 (must) and OT2E-12: a view dated before the start of a running rewatch run was logged and not counted; a start that started nothing was stored as a history row; a start could be dated before the first run's views. What does the server take? | The rules live in `app/src/run_rules.rs` and are checked in `App::log_progress` before anything is written, so the REST call, the sync command (offline queue) and "watched with" obey them; the web is not relied on. (1) While a run is running, an episode of a regular season whose `occurred_at` lies before the start of the run is refused (422 `invalid`, field `occurred_at`). The "Watched with..." dialog refuses the same before it sends: "You started again on <date>. Pick that day or a later one." (en, de), inline at the date chips; a day equal to the day of the start is sent with the start's own moment when noon would lie before it. Specials (season 0) are not part of a run and keep their dates. (2) An episode sent without a date while a run is running is dated `max(now, start + 1 ms)`: a start that came from a queue whose clock runs ahead (up to `CLOCK_SKEW_MINUTES`) no longer puts the next episode before itself. The same floor holds for a start and a stop sent without a date (not before the newest event). (3) OT2E-12, chosen: refuse, do not store. A start on an entry that is not Completed, or on an item that is not in the library (a marker never adds the item), and a stop without an open run answer 409 `conflict`; the offline queue shows it as a refused command like any other. (4) OT2E-12, `occurred_at` on a start, chosen: refuse the bad one, keep the rest. A start dated before the newest event of the item is refused (`invalid`): the views after it would become its run ("Watched 2 times" after one pass). The date is not ignored altogether because a queue sends the start with its own time and its episodes with later ones; a start re-dated to the flush would sort after its own episodes. A stop dated before its run's start is refused the same way. (5) A replay (same op id, DEC-455) answers the stored event before any rule is checked, so a flush sent twice stays a no-op. (6) A source that dates its views itself is the exception to (1): `App::import_progress` stores a view dated before a running run's start (it is old on purpose and is not counted in the run, DEC-587 (7)); the importers and the play of an integration (a media server's webhook with its own `watched_at`) use it, so a late scrobble is not answered with an error. (7) "Watched with" logs its session first: `App::check_progress` asks the same rules before, so a refused view leaves no session. One more indexed read per marker (the entry); episodes on an In progress entry read the history once, as before (OT2E-14, accepted) | (a) of the review only in the web (a script or the queue would still lose views); (b) log it and say in the toast that it does not count (the person still cannot finish the run); move the start back to the earliest view (changes a stored event, D-12); ignore `occurred_at` on a start (breaks the order of a queue, see (4)); store a start that starts nothing and hide it in the history (still an event in the stats) | drop the three arms of `run_rules::occurred_at` (it then answers `sent.unwrap_or(now)`), `App::check_progress` in `api/src/ops.rs::log_progress`, and `log_form::within_run` | docs/reviews/2026-11-11-ot2e-review.md OT2E-1, OT2E-12; DEC-587; DEC-455; tests app `tests/rewatch.rs::{a_view_dated_before_the_start_of_a_running_run_is_refused, a_start_cannot_be_dated_before_the_newest_view, what_is_not_a_run}`, api `rest::a_rewatch_run_refuses_what_it_could_not_count`, web `log_form::tests::a_day_before_the_start_of_a_running_rewatch_is_refused`, `log_sheet::tests::a_day_before_a_rewatch_names_the_day_it_started` (all seen red first), `item.spec.ts`
DEC-592 | Review ot2e, OT2E-5 and OT2E-6: DEC-588 changed when an undo reverts a status move (also when a later undo restored that write). Does that change undo outside a rewatch run, and is it tested? | It does not, and cannot: the chain rule (`StatusChange::carried`) differs from "exactly that write" (DEC-208) only when two events of one entry both moved the status and the later one is undone first. Outside a run no kind has two such events: `finished` is refused for a paged, timed, episodic or sessions kind (`Progress::validate_for`), and for the single model the first `finished` goes straight to Completed. The review's example (a book: a page log, then "Mark as finished", both undone) is not reachable; a book is completed by hand, which writes no event. Inside runs the chain can span several: the undo of a second run's start restores the Completed that the first run's last episode wrote, so that episode can still be undone (the first run runs again) and then the first start. A stop marker (DEC-590) is one more link of the same kind. Tests: `app/tests/undo.rs::an_undo_chain_needs_two_moving_events_and_only_runs_have_them` (the book refuses `finished`, its two page logs undo to Planned as before; two runs taken back newest first); four unit tests of `carried` in `domain/src/progress.rs` (the very write; through two undos; an event stored before `from_stamp` ends the chain; a stamp no undo wrote carries nothing older). They pin existing code and were green from the start. OT2E-6, second half: the domain test of undo no longer asserts a status its own helper computed; `Show::undo` appends the undo event only, the test checks what `runs` reads and names the app test as the one for the status | a hand-written completion event for books so that the review's case exists (a new event type, out of scope: see DEC-594, OT2E-3) | - (tests and wording only) | docs/reviews/2026-11-11-ot2e-review.md OT2E-5, OT2E-6; DEC-588; DEC-208
DEC-593 | Review ot2e, OT2E-7 a, OT2E-8 and OT2E-2 c: the generic export wrote undo events the import refuses (one undone bulk rewatch made the row of that show fail), no test carried a run through export and import, and a run that was running at export time came back without its start. What is in the file, and what does the import do with a run? | (1) The export writes the effective history (`App::history`): an undone log and its undo event are not in the file. This changes what an export contains; nothing a person sees in the app is missing from it. The import keeps refusing the undo type (a file is not a way to undo someone's events). (2) The markers of a run (`rewatch`, `rewatch_stop`) are rows of `history[].progress` like every event. The importer logs through `App::import_progress` (DEC-591 (6)), and before it replays a start it sets the entry to Completed: a start was only ever stored on a Completed entry, so this is the status the entry had, whatever the file's own status says at the end (it still has the last word). With that a run that was running at export time is running after the import; the limit DEC-589 listed is gone. (3) A marker the target account cannot take (it already holds newer views of the item, or the file is not in order) is left out and the row goes on: its views are still imported, the run is not. Trakt and legacy files never contain a marker and import as before. Test `api/tests/rest.rs::export_then_import_keeps_rewatch_runs_and_leaves_undone_logs_out` (two stopped runs in a row, a finished run with an undone extra view, a running run; the other account reads the same status, count, run and number of events; seen red: the file contained `"undo"`). OT2E-7 b: `web/src/offline.rs::queued_log` is now the one place that builds a queued log (the start and the stop of a run, the episodes of a bulk mark); test `offline::tests::a_rewatch_run_is_queued_as_logs_in_their_order`; the queue's replay on the server is `it` `offline::a_rewatch_run_started_offline_replays_once`. DEC-594 | Review ot2e, the remaining findings: applied, deferred or accepted? | OT2E-3 deferred: "Completions" in stats and goals count events that moved an entry to Completed, so a show or a book completed by hand (no event) was never a completion, and a finished rewatch run now is one. This predates ot2e for first runs (0 before, 0 now). A fix needs a decision on what the measure means and an event for a status set by hand; follow-up for the stats package (ot2f or ot2g), not built here. Until then DEC-587 (9) stands: a finished run counts, a stopped one does not (DEC-590). OT2E-4 recorded as a limit of DEC-589: an undo of one episode inside a finished run, or of its start alone, leaves the run finished (the finishing event is still in the effective history: the count, the status and the completion stay). Checking a finished run against the episodes known today would be wrong the other way (a new season would un-finish it). OT2E-9 and OT2E-14 accepted as reviewed, no code; sync.adoc now says that `fraction` and `next_step` count within a running run. OT2E-10 applied: German keeps "beendet" for a finished pass and uses "abbrechen" / "abgebrochen" for a stop ("Wiederholung abbrechen", "... nach 5 von 24 Folgen abgebrochen"); the podcast stop reads "Stop this listen-through"; the finish toast is "Rewatch finished: <title>" / "Wiederholung beendet: <title>", for a podcast "Finished listening again: <title>" where the caller knows the kind (`LoggedStep.verb`; the palette and the cards read like a show). New: toast "Rewatch stopped" / "Wiederholung abgebrochen", history row "Stopped before the end" / "Vorzeitig abgebrochen". These replace the approved texts of ux-owner-test.md 1.3 for the stop: owner's call. DEC-596 | ot2f, OT-11 rest (DEC-539): what is a win rate, whose is it, and how is it read? | The rate is the person's own: wins / plays with a result of theirs. A play has a result when a co-op game was won or lost, or a competitive play names a winner (`Session::decided`); it is theirs when one of their own entries took part (`Session::result_for`: the self entry and every entry merged into it, `App::own_entries`). A co-op win is a win for everyone at the table; a tie counts as a win for each winner (as on the People cards, DEC-383). Not in the rate: a co-op play without a result (DEC-595), a play without a winner, a play the person logged without taking part. Plays and minutes count every session of the item in the person's log. Read: one grouped statement, `SessionRepo::play_totals(owner, mine, item)` (per item: plays, minutes, decided, wins; two lateral subqueries on `session_participants`, the index `sessions_item` of 0031 serves the item filter); the port's default is the same rule in Rust (`ports::fold_play_totals`), which the in-memory store uses and the Postgres test compares the statement with. `App::item_play_totals` (one item) and `App::play_totals` (every item of a kind with the sessions model; "watched with" sessions are no plays). Delivered additively: `ItemPageDto.play_totals` (only for a played kind) and `StatsCompareDto.play` (`PlayTotalsDto {plays, minutes, decided, wins}`). All time, no year filter. The stats cache is not involved: hours in the tiles, totals row and charts still come from it (ot2c) | compute the rate in the browser from `ItemPageDto.plays` (capped at 50, DEC-502); a rate per person at the table (the People cards have it, DEC-383); put the totals into `StatsTotalsDto` (Home would pay for a number it does not show); a new route `GET /stats/plays` (one more pass-through through `Backend`, which WP-B2 wants gone, DEC-544); columns in `stats_months` (a migration and a cache rebuild for a number that is cheap to read) | remove `play_totals` from the port, `store-pg` and `.sqlx`, `app/src/plays.rs`, the two DTO fields and `PlayTotalsDto`; clients ignore missing fields | owner-answers-2026-11-10.md OT-11; ux-owner-test.md 2.4; tests `app/tests/plays.rs`, `store-pg/tests/phase1.rs::play_totals_on_postgres_match_the_rule_in_rust`, `api/tests/rest.rs` `play_totals_are_read`

DEC-603 | Review of ot2f, OT2F-4 (with OT2F-1, 3, 5, 6, 11, 12): is a play of the person alone a result, can a scored play be saved without a winner, and what else changed in the score table? ORCHESTRATOR DEFAULT, THE OWNER MAY REVERSE the first two | (1) A score alone names no winner: the mark follows the highest score only when at least two players have a score, so a solo play (or a play with one score typed) has no result unless the person marks the winner; a win rate counts only plays with a result (DEC-596, unchanged on the server: the dialog simply sends no winner). Before, ten scored solo plays read "100%, 10 of 10". (2) The table has a last row "No winner" with its own radio in the "Won" column: it is checked whenever no player is marked (a tie, fewer than two scores) and can be chosen by hand for a scored play; "Use the highest score" takes any choice by hand back. `log_form::WinnerChoice` (Auto / Nobody / Player) replaces the optional id. (3) OT2F-1: the ids of the score field and the radio come from the companion id (`play-score-<id>`, `play-won-<id>`), not from the row index, which went stale when a player left and another joined (a click on the new row's 36 px target marked the row above). (4) OT2F-3: after "Use the highest score" the keyboard focus goes to the radio that is marked now (or "No winner"). (5) OT2F-5: a winner chosen by hand who leaves the table is no choice any more (the highest score decides again; `PlayForm::winner` ignores an absent id and the dialog resets its state). (6) OT2F-6: a visible hint under the table, tied to it by `aria-describedby` ("The highest score is marked as the winner. Choose another if needed."), and a polite status line that says who is marked when the mark follows the scores ("<name> is marked as the winner (highest score)." / "No winner is marked."). "Lowest score wins" is not built (a setting per game, not trivial); the hint and the radios are the way to say it today. (7) OT2F-11: the table has fixed columns (`table-fixed`; the name takes the rest and is cut), so it fits a 320 px sheet. DEC-605 | Review of ot2f, load-run verdict: the host cannot run the release load run (15 GB of free disk, DEC-399). What is done instead, and where does the run go? | The play read is one statement per case: `play_totals_of_item` (`WHERE s.owner_id = $1 AND s.item_id = $3`) and `play_totals_of_owner` in `store-pg/src/repos/sessions.rs`; the form `($3::uuid IS NULL OR s.item_id = $3)` is gone, so the item filter can use the index `sessions_item` under a generic plan too. `cargo xtask sqlx-prepare` run (two statements in `.sqlx`). EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) on a seeded test database (PostgreSQL 18.6; 45 000 sessions, 134 500 participants; the owner has 5 000 plays, 1 000 of one item, and three own entries in `ANY($2)`; 20 other owners with 2 000 plays each; prepared statements with `plan_cache_mode = force_generic_plan`, warm cache): per item with 1 000 plays: BitmapAnd of `sessions_item` and `sessions_owner_recent`, 9 855 buffers, 14.9 ms; per item with 13 plays: the same plan, 181 buffers, 0.44 ms (the old form on the same item: `sessions_owner_recent` with 4 987 of 5 000 rows removed by the filter, 229 buffers, 0.95 ms); every item (5 000 plays): `sessions_owner_recent`, then two probes of `session_participants_pkey` per session, 49 103 buffers, 74.5 ms. The read over all items is linear in the owner's plays (about 15 ms per 1 000 on this host) and runs on every `GET /stats/compare`; nothing caches it. The full plans are in docs/fable/round2/ot2f-progress.md ("EXPLAIN ANALYZE of the play read"). THE RELEASE LOAD RUN MOVES TO THE PERFORMANCE WORKSTREAM (W2): `GET /stats/compare` and the item page of a game with many plays, with an account that has merged entries; W2 also decides whether the read over all items needs one pass over the participants (a join grouped by session instead of two lateral probes) or a cached total | keep one statement and rely on custom plans (the planner switches to a generic plan when it looks no worse); a composite index `sessions (owner_id, item_id)` (the BitmapAnd of the two existing indexes costs 42 buffers; not needed at this size) | the two functions back into one statement with the `IS NULL OR` filter | docs/reviews/2026-11-11-ot2f-review.md "Load run"; DEC-399; DEC-601; PostgreSQL documentation, plan_cache_mode https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/runtime-config-query.html#GUC-PLAN-CACHE-MODE; test `store-pg/tests/phase1.rs::play_totals_on_postgres_match_the_rule_in_rust` (both statements against the rule in Rust)

DEC-607 | OT-3 step 2 (owner: "Harry Potter and the Order of the Homeless is over the original Harry Potter"): among documents that hold all the words, what orders the local search, and what does it cost? | Postgres fallback (`search/src/pg.rs`): in the `exact` phase the title decides before the text score: `whole` (the first searchable attribute, lower-cased, equals the query), then `lead` (it starts with the query), then `ts_rank + similarity` and the id as before; the typo phase is unchanged and still behind its one-time filter (DEC-493). The text score alone ranked by how short the text is and how often it repeats the words (a title whose original title repeats them scored higher). Meilisearch: no settings change. The server of the dev services (1.56.0) answers `GET /indexes/<uid>/settings/ranking-rules` with `["words","typo","proximity","attributeRank","sort","wordPosition","exactness"]`, and with these the new suite block passes as it is (wordPosition puts a title that starts with the words first, exactness the title that is the query); the diagnosis' proposal to move `exactness` up was written against the older rule list (`attribute`) and is not needed. Not part of this package: popularity (step 3 of the diagnosis); two titles that both start with the query are still ordered by the text score, so the local order is a fallback and the palette lets a source's answer order its group (DEC-609). Test first: `search/tests/suite.rs` `title_ties` on both backends (documents indexed derivative first, original titles repeating the words; "harry potter" and "HARRY POTTER" give the title that is the query first and "The Making of Harry Potter" last; "harry poter" still finds all four): red on Postgres (`["d4","d3","d2","c4"]`), green on Meilisearch before any change. Cost, EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) on PostgreSQL 18.6, databases seeded by SQL like the load test (50 551 catalog documents "<word> <word> <n>", ten words; a second database with the same titles and a description of about 600 characters each, table 174 MB), prepared statements with `plan_cache_mode = force_generic_plan`, all buffers cached, three to five runs each, old statement / new statement in ms. Titles only: "amber cedar" 41.6-42.0 / 41.4-42.1; "cedar ember 4242" 48.7-49.8 / 48.8-50.0; "granit" 55.5-56.5 / 61.4-62.6; "juniper delta 777" 50.6-52.3 / 50.8-52.0; "indigo" 54.1-54.7 / 60.3-62.3. With descriptions: 62.4-64.4 / 62.4-65.3; 68.8-73.8 / 67.7-69.1; 75.6-76.7 / 80.7-81.8; 68.0-76.4 / 68.5-69.2; 72.2-74.0 / 78.1-79.9. So the two one-word queries, which match about 10 000 documents (a fifth of the index) and compute the two expressions for each before the top 20 are cut, cost 6 to 7 ms more (about 11 %); the others are unchanged; the plan is the same (same nodes and row counts). A variant that tests `body` instead of `fields ->> title` was measured and was not faster (61.4-63.4 ms for the one-word queries), so the simpler statement stays. Observation, not followed up: the absolute numbers under a forced generic plan are higher than DEC-493's psql timings (11-31 ms, literal parameters); which plan the application's prepared statements get was not measured. Release load run: not run (the host lacks the 15 GB of disk); judged not required for this change: the last release runs read search p95 84 to 122 ms against the 300 ms budget (DEC-493, DEC-494), the worst case adds 6 to 7 ms to one statement plus 0.13 ms for the library lookup of DEC-609; the next W2 load run will show it | popularity from the providers (step 3: a column, a migration, a connector field per source; not in this package, and DATA-0 changes the identity source first); order by `whole`/`lead` only inside Rust after the read (the page is cut in SQL, so the title that is the query could be cut off); a stored generated column for the lower-cased title (a migration for 6 ms) | restore `ORDER BY score DESC, id` in both places of the statement in `search/src/pg.rs`, drop the two columns and the sixth parameter, run `cargo xtask sqlx-prepare` | package ot1b (`docs/fable/round2/next-packages.md`); `docs/fable/round2/bug-diagnosis.md` OT-3; DEC-493; Meilisearch docs "Built-in ranking rules" https://www.meilisearch.com/docs/learn/relevancy/ranking_rules (read 2026-11-12: the seven rules in this order; "wordPosition: Results are sorted by the position of query terms within the attributes"); PostgreSQL docs 9.4 String Functions (`starts_with`, `lower`), 14.1 EXPLAIN
DEC-608 | OT-2 (owner: "found items have no thumbnail"): which picture may a palette row load? The owner's answers of 2026-11-10 do not rule on hotlinking. The providers' terms do not ask for stored covers: TMDB's "Image basics" page (cited in `docs/connectors/tmdb.md`) describes how "to generate a fully working image URL" on `image.tmdb.org` and restricts nothing about loading it, TVmaze says "You are free to directly link"; what the terms on file restrict is the other direction, stored copies (TMDB terms 1.C, six months). `tvdb.md` and `igdb.md` record no statement either way (corrected after review OT1B-2) | The conservative option of the plan: a row shows a cover only when this instance has stored it (`ImageDto.variants` not empty; the smallest stored variant, WebP when there is one, from our own `/assets/...`); a cover known only by its provider address is not loaded from the palette, the row shows the icon of its kind in a muted box of the same size (2:3, square for music, podcasts and board games). `ui::CommandLeading { icon, image, square }` on `CommandItem`; the slot is 2.5 rem wide whatever the shape, so the labels line up, the icon stays under the picture (it shows while the picture loads and when it fails), the slot is `aria-hidden` and the `img` has `alt=""`, `loading="lazy"`, `referrerpolicy="no-referrer"`: the option's name is still its label. Rows of the idle list (recent items, commands) have no slot, as before. Effect the owner will see: with the default image policy (`assets.fetch_policy = library`) only items of a library, a list or a play have a stored cover, so the person's own titles show their cover and most rows of a source's answer show the kind icon; with `fetch_policy = eager` the covers of search results are stored too and show from the next search on. Reason: privacy, as the conservative default of the plan, not a requirement of the providers' terms: 20 rows per typing pause would send the viewer's address to the provider's image host for titles the person never opened. For the owner's ruling: the pages already do that for covers that are not stored yet. `widgets.rs` `Cover` uses the provider's address as `src` until the asset job has stored the cover, and it is what the item page, the Library cards and Home's Continue and Up next cards render; during an import that is every new card in every open browser of that user. Unchanged here; a ruling "stored covers only" would have to cover those too | (a) load the provider address as the item page does (owner decision: say so and `palette::thumb` falls back to `cover.url`); (c) a small proxied thumbnail endpoint (every palette row would then cost a download and storage; that is what `fetch_policy = eager` already does in the background) | in `palette::thumb` fall back to `cover.url` when no variant is stored | package ot1b; `docs/fable/round2/bug-diagnosis.md` OT-2 (options a, b, c); `docs/connectors/tvmaze.md` (the one hotlink statement on file); tests `ui/tests/wasm.rs` command_option_renders_its_leading_slot, `palette::tests::to_item_keeps_the_cover`, `shell.spec.ts` "palette: the library leads, a source's answer keeps its order, rows lead with a cover box"
DEC-609 | OT-3 step 1 (rest): every hit of every search is kept as a catalog copy, the palette listed those copies first ("In your instance") in the order of our own text score and only added a source's answer below them, so from the second search on (the palette searches at every typing pause) a derivative title could stand above the one the source names first. What leads the palette, and who orders a source's titles? | (1) Only the person's library leads: `SearchResponseDto.in_library` (new, `#[serde(default)]`: the ids of the local hits that are in the searcher's library, in hit order; one read by `(user, items)` through `LibraryRepo::entries_for`, skipped when nothing was found, a failed read names none) and `palette::local_rows` puts those rows first under the new heading `search-library` ("In your library" / "In deiner Bibliothek"). (2) Every other local hit is a kept copy: it is listed in the group of its source (`asked_source`: the source of its title in `provenance`, else of one of its external ids) when this search asks that source, else under "In your instance" as before. (3) `palette::merge`: when a source answers, its rows take the place where its group stood, in the order the source gave them; kept copies the answer does not name follow them; a row already placed (a library row, a row of another source's answer) stays where it is, so nothing moves twice. The active row is remembered by item (DEC-499 and before), so a reordered list keeps it. Cost: one statement more on a search that found something, `SELECT ... FROM library_entries WHERE user_id = $1 AND item_id = ANY($2)` on the unique index `(user_id, item_id)`: 0.13 ms for 20 ids in a library of 50 551 entries (EXPLAIN ANALYZE, generic plan, PostgreSQL 18.6, second run: Index Scan, 23 buffers). OpenAPI: the field is in the generated document (additive). Not done: a row that is in the library but that our index did not find still appears in its source's group, without the heading | flag each item (`CatalogItemDto.in_library`: the DTO is shared by every endpoint and by the events, which go to other users too); let the palette ask the library index itself (a second search per pause); drop the kept copies from the answer altogether (a search with no source asked or all sources down would find nothing the person has not added) | `palette.rs`: `items.set(r.items.iter().map(|i| to_item(i, &local)).collect())` and the old `add_results` (append what is not listed); remove `in_library` from the DTO and `hits_in_library` from `api/src/ops.rs`; remove the key `search-library` | package ot1b; `docs/fable/round2/bug-diagnosis.md` OT-3 (mechanism 3, fix step 1); DEC-493, DEC-499; tests `api/tests/rest.rs` search_names_the_hits_in_the_searchers_library, `palette::tests::library_entries_lead_and_a_source_keeps_its_order`, `shell.spec.ts` "palette: the library leads, a source's answer keeps its order, rows lead with a cover box"
DEC-610 | OT-21 (owner: "while the import was working in the background covers flashed hard (between their cover image and a text saying something about cover); once the import was done it stopped"): why, and what keeps a cover on screen? | Cause, from the code: an import announces every item it writes as `entity.updated`; Home, Library and History refetched on each event, at once; Home rendered all of itself afresh for every answer that differed (`Shown::next`), and a library card was rendered afresh whenever its row changed, so each time every cover was a new `img` (`loading="lazy"`, `decoding="async"`) that showed its box, and in it the alternative text "Cover of <title>", until the picture was painted again. Three changes. (1) Paced refetches: `events::refresh_on_updates` with `events::Pace`: the first event refetches at once (a single change elsewhere shows as fast as before), events in the 2 s after a refetch are answered by one refetch at the end of that time; used by Home, Library and History (the item page keeps its own filter by item and its 300 ms delay; Notifications does not listen to `entity.updated` at all, it refetches on `notification.new`; People, the person page and both list pages refetched for every `entity.updated` through `people::refetch_on_update` and are paced the same way since the review, DEC-614). A refresh after the person's own action is direct and not paced. (2) Cards that outlive answers: `widgets::keyed` (one shell per key for as long as the key is listed, the row in a memo), `StableCover` (rendered again only when title, image or shape change; the element itself is kept by the card around it, the renderer patches an `img` in place, DEC-617); Home's sections read a memo of the latest answer and stay from one answer with rows to the next (`Shown::next` keeps the render key then; an error, the empty library and the way back still render afresh), Continue and Up next cards are keyed by entry; `PosterCard` keeps its `li`, link and cover and renders the acting parts (pill, quick menu, line, tags: `PosterCardRest`) afresh when the row changes, as `ContinueCard` does with `ContinueBody`, so handlers still carry the new data (UX1-2, DEC-398). (3) Neutral placeholder: the cover `img` has `text-transparent`, so no browser paints the alternative text in the box while the picture loads or when it fails (the thumbhash, the dominant colour or the muted box shows); the `alt` attribute is unchanged and stays the picture's name for assistive technology. A cover that fails to load is now an empty muted box (the title stands next to or under every cover) | pace only while `import.progress` events are seen (a second state; a bulk change from another device floods a page the same way); `loading="eager"` and `decoding="sync"` on recreated images (still a new element per answer, and all covers of a long grid would load at once); debounce instead of leading edge (every single change would show 2 s late); a longer quiet time (3 s was considered: a second background change within the quiet time would then show up to 3 s late, close to the 5 s the browser tests wait by default) | `events.rs`: remove `Pace` and call `refresh.run(())` per event in `refresh_on_updates`; `home.rs`: `Shown::next` bumps the key for every new answer and `HomeSections` takes the answer by value; `widgets.rs`: `PosterCard` takes the row by value again; remove `text-transparent` from `Cover` | package ot1b (`docs/fable/round2/next-packages.md`, OT-21); `docs/handoff/owner-test-2026-11-10.md` OT-21; DEC-398, DEC-486, DEC-490; HTML Standard 4.8.3 "The img element" (alternative text is rendered when the image is not available) https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/embedded-content.html#the-img-element ; tests `events::tests::a_burst_of_events_refetches_once_per_quiet_time`, `home::tests::the_sections_outlive_answers_with_rows_and_offline_keeps_the_last_one`, `home.spec.ts` "covers stay while an import runs: cards keep their pictures, refetches are paced"
DEC-611 | Review OT1B-3 (the owner's own case): "Harry Potter and the Order of the Homeless" and "Harry Potter and the Philosopher's Stone" both start with the query, so the title rules of DEC-607 tie and the text score put the derivative first, on both backends (seen in the new test before the code: Postgres `["p2","p1","p3","p4"]`, Meilisearch `["p2","p3","p1","p4"]`). What breaks the tie in our own index, and which provider gives a usable signal? | The source's popularity, stored with the item and used after the title rules. Sources, read and not guessed: TMDB's search and detail answers carry `popularity` (number) and `vote_count` (integer) for movies and shows (reference "Search > Movie", read 2026-11-12: `"popularity": {"type": "number"}`, `"vote_count": {"type": "integer"}`; our fixtures are TMDB's example answers and hold both). `vote_count` is taken: it only grows and separates an original (tens of thousands) from a derivative (a handful) by orders of magnitude, while `popularity` is made of the day's activity ("Number of votes for the day", "Number of views for the day", "Previous days score") and a kept copy would hold the value of one day for up to six months. TheTVDB: the search answer (`SearchResult`, swagger.yml 4.8.0) has no such field; `score` exists on the detail record only and the spec says "no assumptions should be made about the meaning of this value. It is simply used to hint at relative popularity for sorting purposes": not read, its scale cannot stand in one list with a count of ratings. IGDB: the API docs answered 403 again on 2026-11-12, so no field is used on a guess. Podcast Index: nothing in the answers on file. Built: migration 0033 `catalog_items.popularity double precision` (additive, NULL = unknown); `CatalogItem`, `NewCatalogItem`, `CatalogRefresh`, `Snapshot` and `Merged` carry `popularity`; the TMDB connector sets it in search and details; `ensure_catalog_item` (search results) fills a missing value on a kept copy and leaves a stored one alone, so a search writes nothing for a copy that has one; a refresh replaces it; a purge (TMDB terms 1.C) clears it. The catalog search document carries `popularity`. Postgres (`search/src/pg.rs`): `ORDER BY whole, lead, pop DESC NULLS LAST, score, id` in the `exact` phase and in the outer order. Meilisearch 1.56 (`search/src/meili.rs`): `popularity:desc` is appended to the ranking rules of the catalog index at connect, after the server's own list (read with `get_ranking_rules`, so the built-in rules stay as the version has them). The Meilisearch page on custom ranking rules says to place them after the built-in rules, that the attribute "must have either a numeric or a string value in all of the documents" and that for a document without it "the application of the ranking rule is undefined": the indexer therefore writes `popularity` as a number into every catalog document, 0 when the source gives none (on Postgres 0 sorts after every real value as well), and documents written before this change need one `cli reindex` (operations.adoc, "Upgrade notes"). Also `pg::weighted` leaves out an original title equal to the title, which doubled the text score of exactly those documents. LIMITS: (1) the tie-break works for copies that carry a value. Copies kept before this change have none; they get it the next time TMDB names them in a search answer or refreshes them, and until then their order is the old one. No backfill was built. (2) Only TMDB gives a value and values of different sources would not be comparable: among titles the title rules leave equal, a TMDB title with a value stands before a game, a book or a TheTVDB show. (3) The typo phase of the Postgres search has no popularity term. Cost, EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) on PostgreSQL 18.6, the seed of DEC-607 (50 551 catalog documents, half of them with a popularity; a second database with 600-character descriptions, 194 MB), prepared statements, `plan_cache_mode = force_generic_plan`, warm, four runs, statement of DEC-607 / final statement (this decision and DEC-612 together) in ms. Titles only: "amber cedar" 41.9-42.5 / 42.9-43.4; "cedar ember 4242" 49.5-51.1 / 49.2-50.2; "granit" 62.3-63.7 / 72.8-74.1; "juniper delta 777" 51.5-51.9 / 49.9-51.4; "indigo" 59.1-59.8 / 69.3-70.1. With descriptions: 65.6-68.1 / 67.3-68.2; 71.6-73.6 / 71.3-72.9; 85.0-86.3 / 98.0-100.6; 70.4-72.6 / 70.8-73.8; 80.6-81.6 / 91.8-92.8. So +10 to 13 ms on the two one-word queries that match 9 600 documents, nothing on the others; the scan and join nodes are the same (Seq Scan with the join filter, top-N heapsort), two Subquery Scan nodes are added (the `OFFSET 0` that keep the title expressions computed once per row). Of the added time the popularity term is about 1 ms (measured without the article rule: 62.7-63.7 and 59.7-59.9 ms), the article rule of DEC-612 the rest. With DEC-607 the two broad queries now cost about 17 to 20 ms more than on HEAD, against a search p95 of 84 to 122 ms and a budget of 300 ms in the last release runs (DEC-493, DEC-494); the release load run was not run (15 GB of disk) and stays with W2 | TMDB `popularity` instead of `vote_count` (the day's activity, stale in a kept copy; a trending derivative can beat an old original); TheTVDB `score` from the details (another scale, meaning not defined by the source); popularity before `lead` (a well-known title that only holds the words later would pass the title that starts with them); the year, oldest first, as the tie-break when no source gives a signal (needs nothing from a provider, wrong for remakes and re-releases; the fallback to take if the popularity is dropped); a backfill job that asks TMDB for every kept copy (one detail call per copy at 20 per second; a follow-up if the owner's existing copies should be ordered before they are named again); a `jsonb_typeof` guard around the cast (3 ms more on the broad queries; the indexer is the only writer and writes a number or null) | `pg.rs`: remove `pop` from the select lists and the two ORDER BY, run `cargo xtask sqlx-prepare`; `meili.rs`: remove `popularity_rule` and reset the rules of the catalog index (`DELETE /indexes/<uid>/settings/ranking-rules`); the column, the fields and the document field may stay (unused) or a migration drops the column | docs/reviews/2026-11-12-ot1b-review.md OT1B-3; TMDB reference https://developer.themoviedb.org/reference/search-movie and https://developer.themoviedb.org/docs/popularity-and-trending (read 2026-11-12); TheTVDB spec https://raw.githubusercontent.com/thetvdb/v4-api/main/docs/swagger.yml (4.8.0, read 2026-11-12); Meilisearch docs "Custom ranking rules" https://www.meilisearch.com/docs/learn/relevancy/custom_ranking_rules (read 2026-11-12); tests `search/tests/suite.rs` popularity_breaks_title_ties (red first on both backends), `connectors/tests/tmdb.rs`, `app/tests/catalog_audience.rs` a_search_result_fills_a_missing_popularity_and_keeps_a_stored_one, `store-pg/tests/retention.rs`, `search/tests/indexer.rs`
DEC-612 | Review OT1B-4: since DEC-607 a title with a leading article ranks below every title that starts with the bare word on the Postgres backend ("avengers": "The Avengers" after "Avengers: Endgame", "Avengers Grimm" and the rest). Skip articles? | On Postgres yes: `whole` and `lead` also compare the lower-cased title without one leading article, English the, a, an and German der, die, das, ein, eine (one `regexp_replace` per matching row, pattern `^(the|a|an|der|die|das|ein|eine) `); the title as written is compared too, so "The Thing" is still the title for the query "the thing". The article of the query is not removed. The two languages are the locales the product ships. A German title that starts with the English word "a" or "die" as a word of another language ("Die Hard") is treated as if it had an article: "hard" finds "Die Hard" among the titles that start with the word, which is harmless. Meilisearch, recorded by the same test and NOT changed: its `wordPosition` rule ranks by the position of the word, so "The Avengers" comes after the titles that start with the word and level with "Lego Avengers" (`["a2","a3","a4","a1"]` in the suite), and "Pate wider Willen" comes before "Der Pate". Making Meilisearch follow needs a normalised title attribute in the document and in `searchableAttributes` (a reindex); not built, the owner's instance decides by its backend. Cost: about 9 to 12 ms on a query that matches 9 600 documents, see DEC-611 | a normalised title field written by the indexer and searched first on both backends (one rule for both, a reindex, a second title in every document); more languages (le, la, les, el, los, las, il: no locale for them yet, and "la" and "die" are also words of titles in other languages); compare from the query side with a list of "article + query" strings (measured, not faster: 67 to 71 ms against 70 to 74) | `pg.rs`: compare `raw` only in `whole` and `lead`, drop the `titled` subquery, run `cargo xtask sqlx-prepare` | docs/reviews/2026-11-12-ot1b-review.md OT1B-4; test `search/tests/suite.rs` leading_articles (Postgres: seen red in a mutation run with a pattern that never matches, `found[0]` "a3"; Meilisearch: records what it does); PostgreSQL docs 9.7.3 POSIX regular expressions (`regexp_replace`)
DEC-613 | Review OT1B-5, OT1B-7, OT1B-8: since DEC-609 the palette listed the kept copies at once in our order inside their source's group and reordered the group when the source answered, so the first row (the active one while nothing is chosen) changed under Enter and Ctrl+Enter, rows below shifted under a resting pointer, a group could get its heading two or three times, and every batch rebuilt every row with its cover. What may move? | Nothing that is on screen. (1) Hold: the copies of a source that this search asks are not listed with our own answer; they wait for that source's answer (`palette::local_rows` returns them apart, `HOLD_MS` = 700 ms). The answer then lists its titles once, in the source's order, followed by the held copies it does not name. First paint: the library rows, then "In your instance" (copies of sources that are not asked, hand-entered items). While copies are held, the answers that arrive wait with them (`palette::Hold`), and everything is listed in one addition as soon as every held group was answered, or after the hold time: the groups in the order of our own hits, then the groups our index did not hit in the order they arrived; inside a group the source's order, then the copies it does not name, in our order, which since DEC-611 is the popularity order. Without that wait the group of whichever source answered first stood first (seen in the browser test: a TV group above the films the search was for). A source answers once per kind and the event does not say which kind, so an answer takes the held rows of the groups it names; an empty or failed answer names none and its copies wait for the hold time. A search without kept copies of an asked source holds nothing: its answers are listed as they come, as before. (2) Add, never move: `palette::merge` leaves every row on screen where it is; new rows keep the order they come in and go to the end of their group when that is below the pinned rows, else to the end of the list. Pinned is everything down to the active row, the first row while nothing is chosen (`palette::pinned`; `ui::Command` tells the palette the active item through the new optional prop `active_item`). In the one case where the active row is below the group that gets a late row, the group has a second heading at the end of the list: a row under the keys or the pointer does not move for the sake of a heading. (3) One heading per group otherwise: `palette::contiguous` keeps each group in one run (OT1B-7). (4) `ui::Command` keys its rows (`<For>` by item id, label, heading and leading), so a row that stays keeps its elements and its cover is not loaded and painted again per batch; the active mark follows the item id (OT1B-8). DEC-426, 499, 500, 503 and 508 hold as written: Ctrl+Enter asks `item_page` at key time, the stream buffer is untouched, a chosen row is remembered by item, `filter_as_typed` and `follow_chosen` are unchanged; their tests pass. Effect the owner sees: the titles of a source appear when it answers (or after 0.7 s), not a moment earlier in another order; the list only grows | show the held copies dimmed and not selectable (a second state to explain and to announce); no hold, append the answer below the copies (the derivative stays first whenever a copy exists); a longer hold (the copies of a slow source would stay hidden while the list looks finished); always append at the end without looking at the active row (a group's heading would repeat whenever another group came between) | `palette.rs`: `local_rows` lists the held rows with the others, `merge` back to the version of DEC-609; `command.rs`: the plain closure over `filtered` instead of `<For>` | docs/reviews/2026-11-12-ot1b-review.md OT1B-5, 7, 8; tests `palette::tests::library_entries_lead_and_a_source_keeps_its_order`, `palette::tests::a_late_answer_adds_rows_below_the_active_row`, `palette::tests::answers_wait_while_copies_are_held_and_keep_the_order_of_our_hits` (all three seen red in mutation runs), `shell.spec.ts` "palette: the library leads, a source's answer keeps its order, rows lead with a cover box" (red on the first build of the review fixes: the first row was a TV title), `ui/tests/wasm.rs` command_rows_keep_their_elements_when_the_list_grows (seen red in a mutation run), `ui` command::tests::a_heading_leads_each_run_of_a_group

The effect does not count the event it finds when the page is built (the page has just loaded). `people::refetch_on_update` now calls `refresh_on_updates`, so People, the person page and the two list pages are paced like Home, Library and History (one refetch at once, then one per 2 s while events keep coming) | a refetch for every own action and one more for its event (two requests per action); pass a refresh callback from every page into the palette (the palette does not know the page behind it); leave People and the lists unpaced (500 `list_page` calls during a 500-item import) | `events.rs`: remove `OwnAction`, `Pace::own` and the first effect of `refresh_on_updates`, drop the `built` check; `next_step.rs`: `StepCtx::done` back to running `on_done` only; `people.rs`: the old effect on `LastEvent` | docs/reviews/2026-11-12-ot1b-review.md OT1B-9, OT1B-11; DEC-610; test `events::tests::an_own_action_counts_as_a_refetch` (the effects themselves have no unit test; the browser suite covers the pages)

DEC-619 | v1-ops, OPS-3 (owner ruling 2026-11-12, option a, HANDOFF 4.8): where does the secrets key live once it is "out of the blob directory", and how does the owner's existing instance keep working? | The key stays where it is, `/data/secrets.key` in the one `app-data` volume; the blob store moves from `/data` to `/data/blobs` (the layout of the application defaults, `data/blobs` next to `data/secrets.key`). New setting `storage.fs_legacy_root` (`APP__STORAGE__FS_LEGACY_ROOT`, empty by default; the image sets `/data`): when the fs backend opens, each of the directories `up`, `img`, `http` found there is renamed into `fs_root` unless `fs_root` already has it; nothing is merged, nothing else is touched, the same directory for both settings moves nothing, a failed rename stops the start with both paths. So the new image on an old volume moves three directories once (a rename inside the volume, logged) and reads every object as before; the storage index in Postgres holds relative keys and needs no change. Tested in `assets::backend::tests` (objects readable after the move, key file and other entries left, second start a no-op, an existing target wins). Not chosen: a second volume or path for the key. An old compose file or a hand-written `docker run` with the new image would then generate the key into the container layer, and the key code (load, reload, rotate, prune, CLI) would need a second place to look; with one volume there is no way to lose the key by upgrading. The image also declares `VOLUME /data` and `WORKDIR /` (an empty `APP__SECRETS__KEY_FILE=` copied from `.env.example` resolves to `/data/secrets.key` as before). `rm -rf /data/*` still removes the key: the docs say to empty `/data/blobs` only | key to `/keys/secrets.key` on a volume of its own with a read-in-place fallback; a documented manual move only (uploads would look lost until it is done); deriving the earlier root from the key file's directory without a setting (a heuristic that also fires outside the image) | set `APP__STORAGE__FS_ROOT=/data` (old layout, the key inside the blob directory again); remove the setting and the function `adopt_legacy_root` | docs/HANDOFF.md 4.8; docs/fable/round2/owner-answers-2026-11-10.md 11.6; ops-readiness.md 2.3 and 1.6; crates/assets/src/backend.rs

DEC-643 | Review of v1-ops, V1OPS-5 (and the code side of V1OPS-3): old and new image on one volume see different blob roots. Who may move the blob directories, and how do all containers get the same image? | (1) Only a start of the server moves them. `assets::legacy_dirs_pending` names the directories of `storage.fs_legacy_root` that a start would move and changes nothing; the admin CLI asks it before it opens the store, and `cli storage report` / `cli storage evict` stop with the directories and "start the server of this image first" while the answer is not empty. A check, not a lock: a container of another image cannot be seen from inside this one, but "the directories are still in the earlier root" is exactly the state in which an older server may be running. (2) One image per project: the `app` service builds and names it (`image: ${APP_IMAGE:-localhost/tracker-app:local}`), the commented `worker` service runs that image (`pull_policy: never`, no `build:` of its own); the runbook builds with `dc build` (no service name) and says that `dc up -d` replaces web process and workers together and that the dry run is the only command to run from the new image before it. (3) The role of the app container is a variable (`command: ["--role=${APP_ROLE:-all}"]`), so "web beside workers" is a line in `config/.env`, not an edit of the compose file. (4) The four top-level prefixes are one list, `assets::prefix::ALL` (`up`, `img`, `http`, `orig`), used by the key builders of the crate, by `classify` and by the move; `orig` (kept originals) was written but not moved. | let only `--role` processes move and have the CLI open the store without moving (it would then report and evict against an empty `/data/blobs` while the objects lie in `/data`); a lock file on the volume (an older image does not know it); `image: tracker-app` without a variable (a second project for a restore rehearsal would retag the live image); the worker keeps its own `build:` and the runbook alone says `dc build` (the reviewer's second option: nothing in the file would then stop a worker of another build) | remove the call of `refuse_pending_move` in apps/cli/src/main.rs; give the worker its `build:` back and drop `image:` from `app` | docs/reviews/2026-11-12-v1-ops-review.md V1OPS-3, V1OPS-5; crates/assets/src/backend.rs; apps/cli/src/main.rs; src/docker/compose.yml; xtask/tests/ops.rs `every_container_runs_the_image_the_app_service_builds`; DEC-619
DEC-644 | Review of v1-ops, V1OPS-1, V1OPS-2, V1OPS-4, V1OPS-10: what does the runbook say for the owner's existing instance, for a restore rehearsal and for the way back, and what of it was run? | operations.adoc has three new sections. "Coming from a build before 0034": on the old checkout create `config/.env`, read the two passwords from the running container (`printenv APP__DATABASE__URL APP__SEARCH__MEILI_MASTER_KEY`) and write them into the file (`POSTGRES_PASSWORD=app`, `MEILI_MASTER_KEY=change-me-please-32-bytes-minimum` when nothing was ever set: an existing database keeps its password), set `APP_PUBLISH_ADDR` when the proxy is on another host, take the backup with the old upload path (`tar -C /data -cf - up`), only then `git pull`; changing the password afterwards is `dc stop app`, `ALTER USER app PASSWORD`, the file, `dc up -d`. "Restore rehearsal beside the live instance": a second compose project (`-p tracker-restore`) with its own host port (`APP_PUBLISH_PORT`, new variable, default 3000), its own image name (`APP_IMAGE`) and `APP_ROLE=web` (a second worker on a copy of the data would send every due notification again), checked with `healthz`, `cli secrets status` and a row count, removed with `down -v`; the Restore section no longer names "remove the volumes" as a way to an empty instance. "Going back": stop the application, recreate the database (`dropdb --force`, `createdb`) and `pg_restore` without `--clean` (it drops only what the dump contains, so a table of a newer migration would stay), move the four blob directories back for a build before 0034, then check out, build, start; the 0034 note has the manual reverse. Restore uses the same recreate-then-restore and `dc up -d --wait postgres meilisearch`. Run on the build host: the database sequence (dump of a database migrated to 0034, `dropdb --force --if-exists --maintenance-db=postgres`, `createdb`, `pg_restore --no-owner` into a database that held an extra table, and the manual reverse of 0034) against the local Postgres 18.6, all with exit code 0. Not run: every `docker compose` command, `-p`, `--wait`, the shell precedence over `--env-file`, `printenv` in the old image, the `mv` loop, a changed Meilisearch master key on existing data. The xtask ops tests check that the pages and files say these things, in this order. | `APP_PUBLISH_ADDR=127.0.0.2` instead of a port variable (the reviewer's example; loopback aliases other than 127.0.0.1 are a Linux habit); tell the owner to generate new passwords and change the database first (one more thing that can go wrong before the backup exists); keep `pg_restore --clean --if-exists` for the plain restore (right for an empty database, wrong once the instance was started by mistake) | the sections are text; `APP_PUBLISH_PORT` falls back to 3000 when unset | docs/reviews/2026-11-12-v1-ops-review.md V1OPS-1, 2, 4, 10; https://hub.docker.com/_/postgres (POSTGRES_PASSWORD only initializes an empty data directory); https://docs.docker.com/compose/how-tos/project-name/ ; https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/app-pgrestore.html ; xtask/tests/ops.rs; DEC-621, DEC-625
