DEC-647 | sec0, SEC-2: which body limit do server functions (`/_fn/*`) and the pages get, and is axum's `DefaultBodyLimit` enough for `leptos_axum::handle_server_fns_with_context` (open in v1-plan.md)? | Found first (the red run of `apps/server/tests/it/body_limit.rs`): the audit's "a body of any size" does not hold for `server_fn` 0.8.13. It reads the body with `into_limited_body()` (`server_fn-0.8.13/src/request/axum.rs`), which honours `DefaultBodyLimit` and without one axum's built-in 2 MiB (`axum-core-0.5.6/src/ext_traits/request.rs`, `DEFAULT_LIMIT`). So a server function already stopped at 2 MiB, but only after reading that much, and answered `500` with the reader's text ("Deserialization|length limit exceeded") whether or not the request declared its length. Choice: `server::body_limit`, an axum middleware on the pages router (server functions, every Leptos page, the fallback for unknown paths and `/pkg`), inside `guarded` (the cross-site guard and the request id run first) and before the session is resolved. Limit `api::JSON_BODY_LIMIT` (2 MiB), the same number as for a JSON request to REST. A declared `Content-Length` above it is answered `413` (`{"code":"too_large"}`) with nothing of the body read; a body without a declared length is read up to the limit and then answered `413`; otherwise the collected body is handed on. The limit is ours now and does not depend on what a library does with a long body. No new crate: `futures` moved from a dev to a normal dependency of `server`; `tower-http` is in the tree only through `leptos_axum` and `reqwest` and was not made a direct dependency. The largest legitimate server-function body is the bulk undo token (`app::MAX_UNDO_BYTES` = 1 MiB before sealing; `api::bulk::tests::the_largest_undo_fits_the_request_body_limit` holds it under the limit), next a bulk request of `app::MAX_BULK` = 500 ids; no server function takes bytes or a stream (110 `#[server]` functions, all with the default encoding), imports upload through REST with `IMPORT_BODY_LIMIT` (64 MiB; guard test in the same file). The other routes: REST has `DefaultBodyLimit` on its router and reads raw bodies with `to_bytes(.., JSON_BODY_LIMIT)` (`oidc_routes.rs`); `/healthz`, `/assets`, `/brand/fonts` are GET routes that never read a body | `DefaultBodyLimit::max(JSON_BODY_LIMIT)` on the pages router alone (makes the number explicit but keeps the late `500` and its text; decided by the red run); `tower_http::limit::RequestBodyLimitLayer` (answers `413` for a declared length only, a chunked body still ends as the library's `500`; a new direct dependency); one limit layer over the whole app (the import upload needs 64 MiB) | remove the `.layer(axum::middleware::from_fn(body_limit))` line and the function in `apps/server/src/lib.rs`; the library's own 2 MiB stays | docs/fable/round2/v1-plan.md section 1; security audit SEC-2 (`wip/fable-reviews:docs/fable/security-audit.md`); tests `body_limit::*`
DEC-648 | sec0, SEC-9: what does a webhook sender get for a storage failure, and where else did the storage text go? | `IntegrationError::Port` now maps through `From<PortError> for ApiError`: status 500, code `internal`, text "internal error". The neighbouring arm `IntegrationError::App` was already generic for `AppError::Repo`; its other texts ("... not found", "forbidden", "conflict: ...", a domain rule) come from the application and hold no SQL or path. The three places that answer the generic 500 (`AppError::Repo`, an auth store error, a port error) share `ApiError::internal`, which logs the detail together with the request id of `request_meta` (the `x-request-id` of the answer). Found beyond the plan: the integration's card in Settings > Integrations and the `integration.call` event took `IntegrationError::to_string()` for a failed call, so the token's owner read "integration storage: <store text>" or "storage error: <store text>" there. `integrations::card_error` now says "internal error" for `Port` and `App(Repo)` and keeps every other text (a refused body still names what is wrong) | keep the text for the owner of the token on the card (the same person may be the sender; the audit names "the owner of one" as the reader to keep it from); a new error code for webhook storage failures (nothing a sender can act on) | `crates/api/src/hooks.rs` (the `Port` arm), `crates/integrations/src/lib.rs` (`card_error` back to `to_string`) | security audit SEC-9; tests `rest::a_storage_failure_behind_the_webhook_answers_with_the_generic_text`, `integrations::tests::the_card_keeps_no_storage_error_text`. Not tested: the log line itself (no subscriber in the api tests)
DEC-649 | sec0, the flaky `notifications.spec.ts` ("Last delivery failed" not visible after a reload): where is the race, and how does Settings > Notifications follow a delivery? | Cause: the page read its data once and read again only for `notification.new`, which is published when the inbox row is written, before the deliveries are queued. The outcome of a delivery (written by the worker later) was announced to nobody, so an open page kept "Never used" / "Queued" until it was loaded again. The spec reloads until the log shows one failed row; with two channels the page could be read between the two deliveries, and the second channel's card then never changed. Second gap: what is published between the server-side read of a page load and the opening of that page's event stream reaches nobody. Choice: (1) `notify::DELIVERY_TOPIC` = `notification.delivery`, payload `{"channel_id"}`, audience the channel's owner; the `Deliverer` publishes it after every outcome it writes (each attempt of a single delivery, also one that is retried or suppressed; a digest once for all its rows). (2) `web::events::StreamOpens` counts the openings of the event stream (the first one and every reconnect); `events::refresh_on_topics(topics, read_at, refresh)` runs `refresh`, paced like DEC-610/614, for the topics and whenever the stream has opened since the page's latest read began (`read_at`: the number of openings at that moment, written by the page when a read begins; 0 for data that came with the page from the server, so a freshly loaded page reads once more when its stream opens and a page reached inside the app does not). (3) The page reads again for both and shows the result in place: `NotifyState::live` holds the health per channel and the log rows of the later read, the cards and the log read it (log rows keyed by id, state, attempts and error, so an unchanged row keeps its nodes). The page is not built anew for it, because a rebuild drops a half-filled "Add a channel" form and folds an unfolded error; only if the later read differs in something else (inbox, channels, rules) the page is refetched as after any change (`notifications::later`). Reads are numbered as they begin, and of two answers the one whose read began later is shown (`page_read`, `live.0`). `notification.new` refetches the page as before. Seen in `reactive_graph-0.2.16` (`arc_async_derived.rs`): a resource runs its reads one after the other, a refetch asked for during a read starts when that read has answered. Test change that is not a wait: the first spec took "the first failed row" twice; the log now follows the second delivery, which sorts above the first, so the spec names its row by the new `data-delivery` attribute of a log row. A second flake of the same spec, found by `--repeat-each=10` and older than this package: "a channel is edited in place ..." starts an import and then expects the page to open on Channels, but the page opens on the inbox when "Import finished" is already unread; the test now opens the Channels tab itself. Not changed: Home, Library and the other pages of `refresh_on_updates` have the same gap between their first read and the opening of the stream for `entity.updated`; a read per page load for them is a cost to weigh, listed as out of scope | refetch the whole page for every delivery and every opening of the stream (one read and one rebuild right after every load of the page, while the person starts typing; an unfolded error folds when the second delivery finishes); replay missed events on the stream with `Last-Event-ID` (needs an event log on the server); poll (D-6.1: nothing polls); a longer timeout or a retry in the spec (hides the stale page a person would see too) | `crates/notify/src/deliver.rs` (remove `announce`), `crates/web/src/notifications.rs` (remove `live`, `later`, `refresh_deliveries` and the `refresh_on_topics` call), `notify_channels.rs` (`health` from the card's own DTO), `events.rs` (`StreamOpens`, `refresh_on_topics`) | v1-ops gate (one failure, rerun green); tests `notify` `engine::a_written_outcome_is_announced_to_the_channels_owner`, `web` `notifications::tests::a_finished_delivery_is_shown_in_place_and_other_changes_build_the_page_anew`, `notifications.spec.ts` "a delivery that fails while the page is open ..." and "a delivery that failed before the page's event stream opened ..." (both red on a build without the fix)
DEC-650 | Review of sec0, SEC0-1 and SEC0-8 (corrects DEC-647 and DEC-648): where else did store text reach a client, and which two sentences of those entries were wrong? | SEC0-1: the notifications REST routes had their own `port` helper in `crates/api/src/notifications.rs` that answered `500` with the text of `notify::pg` ("notifications db: <sqlx error>") on 22 call sites and logged nothing. The helper is gone; the call sites use `From<PortError> for ApiError` (`ApiError::internal`: fixed text "internal error", the detail in the log with the request id). So DEC-648's "the three places that answer the generic 500 share `ApiError::internal`" was wrong when written: there was a fourth place that did not; now the conversions `AppError::Repo`, an auth store error and every `PortError` (webhook, integrations, notifications) go through it. Read for the same leak: every `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` of `crates/api/src` and `apps/server/src` and every `map_err` that formats an error. The others already answer a fixed text or no body and log the detail, without the request id: `admin/mod.rs` (`port`, `pe`, `job_err`, storage report, eviction), `ops.rs` (`import_error`), `sso_routes.rs` (back-channel logout), `apps/server/src/lib.rs` (assets). `admin::reload_keys` and `admin::send_mail` return a `String` that only the log gets. A channel test and the admin's mail test show what the channel or the SMTP server answered, which is their purpose. Server functions pass the error code only. SEC0-8: DEC-647's "110 `#[server]` functions, all with the default encoding" is wrong: 12 of the 110 take `input = Json` (`crates/web/src/api.rs` 7, `admin_api.rs` 5; among them the bulk request of up to 500 ids). The conclusion of DEC-647 stands (none takes bytes or a stream, the largest body is the sealed undo token, the limit is the number the library applied before), but a rule per content type has to count the JSON functions | make the admin helpers use `ApiError::internal` too (they do not leak; the request id in their log lines belongs to the request span named in the progress log); keep `port` and only change its body (one more name for the same conversion) | `crates/api/src/notifications.rs`: `.map_err(ApiError::from)` back to a helper | docs/reviews/2026-11-12-sec0-review.md SEC0-1, SEC0-8; test `rest::a_storage_failure_behind_the_notifications_routes_answers_with_the_generic_text` (a `NotifyStore` whose every call fails; red: the body held the store text)
DEC-651 | Review of sec0, SEC0-3, SEC0-4, SEC0-5 (replaces the page part of DEC-649): how does Settings > Notifications take a later read without wiping a form? | The page is built once. `NotifyState::page` (a signal) holds the latest read, and every part reads it: the unread count of the tab, the inbox (days and rows keyed, a row is replaced only when it is marked read), the channel cards (keyed by id; name, target, "off" badge, health and the failing box follow the signal; the edit form is built from the channel when "Edit" is pressed and stays until saved or cancelled), the rules (built from events, rules and channels without their health, so a delivery or an inbox row does not touch them; the form "Add a rule for one list, item or person" is built once and its channel choices follow the signal) and the log (rows keyed by the delivery id alone; state badge, error text and channel name are reactive cells, so a retried row keeps its unfolded error and the focus: SEC0-5). Every cause of a read goes the same way, `NotifyState::refresh` (read, then `show`): a change made on the page (at once), `notification.new`, `notification.delivery`, `stream.lagged` and an opening of the event stream (paced, `events::refresh_on_topics`). Reads are numbered as they begin and the answer of the read that began later is shown; an answer is kept whether or not the page is built yet (SEC0-4: the first read of a page reached inside the app can be answered after a later one). Gone: `Live`, `Later`, `later`, `refresh_deliveries`, the refetch of the page's resource (it is read once; "Retry" after a failed first read still refetches it). After "Add rule" the form keeps its choices and only the subject is reset (the page used to be built anew, which reset all of it). Not changed: the rules matrix is still built anew when a rule or a channel changes, so the switch that was pressed loses the focus as before; listed as out of scope. The test precondition in "a channel is edited in place ..." (it opens the Channels tab itself) stays: which tab opens first depends on whether the import the test started has finished | the review's smaller fix (wait for the import in the test, record the app defect): leaves the defect for a person who fills the form while any notification arrives; refetch the page and restore form values and focus afterwards (two sources of truth for every field); keep `live` for deliveries and add a second one for the inbox (two mechanisms for one thing) | `crates/web/src/{notifications,notify_channels,notify_rules_view}.rs` from HEAD plus DEC-649 | docs/reviews/2026-11-12-sec0-review.md SEC0-3, SEC0-4, SEC0-5; `notifications.spec.ts` "an open form keeps its text and the focus when an inbox row arrives and after a save on the page" (red on the build before: the name of the new channel was empty after the inbox row); web `notifications::tests::the_rules_do_not_see_what_a_delivery_changes`
DEC-652 | Review of sec0, SEC0-2: the health of a channel now changes on its own while the page is open; how does assistive technology learn of it (WCAG 4.1.3)? | One visually hidden paragraph with `role="status"` (polite) on the page. When a later read changes the health state of a channel that is shown to "failing" or to "working", it gets one sentence per channel: "<channel>: the last delivery failed" / "<channel>: the last delivery got through" (`notify-live-failing`, `notify-live-working`, en and de). Nothing is said for the first read, for a channel that is new, for a state that did not change (a second failure in a row) or for "waiting to send". The failing box of the card keeps `role` none: it is also there on a normal page load and would be read on every load as an alert | `role="alert"` on the card's box (read on every page load); `aria-live` on each card (the whole card text is read again, also for its own "Send test" result, which has its own alert); a toast (the page uses inline results, R-38) | remove the paragraph in `Notifications` and `health_news` in `crates/web/src/notifications.rs` | docs/reviews/2026-11-12-sec0-review.md SEC0-2; `notifications.spec.ts` "a delivery that fails while the page is open ..." (`getByRole("status")`, red on the build before); web `notifications::tests::a_later_read_says_whose_health_changed`
DEC-653 | Review of sec0, SEC0-6: `body_limit` collects up to 2 MiB for every path of the pages router and has no read timeout; what is done now, what later? | Now: a body that cannot be read (the sender broke off) is answered `400` in the `ErrorDto` shape (`bad_request`, "the request body could not be read") instead of a bare status. Deferred to package sec2 (throttling and timeouts): a read timeout for a body that stalls, and with it the narrower collecting the review proposes (collect only for `/_fn/`, hand other bodies on behind a length limit). Both answer the same scenario (many connections that send almost 2 MiB and then stall), and without the timeout the narrower collecting only moves where the stalled bytes are held; a server-wide header and body read timeout is one setting of the listener that sec2 has to choose together with its limits per address. Until then the exposure is as the review states it: about 2 MiB per stalled connection before a session is looked up, new for page paths, unchanged for `/_fn/*` | add `tower_http::timeout::RequestBodyTimeoutLayer` now (a new direct dependency and a number chosen without sec2's limits; one timeout layer of the pages router alone would leave REST without one); collect only for `/_fn/` now (half of the answer, see above) | `apps/server/src/lib.rs` `body_limit`: the `bad_request` line back to `StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST.into_response()` | docs/reviews/2026-11-12-sec0-review.md SEC0-6; test `body_limit::a_body_that_breaks_off_is_answered_as_an_error_document` (written with the change, not seen red); docs/fable/round2/v1-plan.md (sec2)
DEC-654 | Review of sec0, SEC0-9: the server sends `stream.lagged` in place of events a slow reader missed (`realtime::events_for`), and no page listened to it; what do the pages do? | `stream.lagged` is one of the topics of the page's event source (`web::events::TOPICS`, `LAGGED`). Every page that follows events reads its data again once, paced like any change from elsewhere (`events::Pace`, at most one read per 2 s): Home, Library, History, People and the lists through `refresh_on_updates`, Settings > Notifications through `refresh_on_topics` (both by `events::concerns`), the item page (its own refetch, which collects events for 300 ms), Settings > Imports and Settings > Integrations through the new `events::refresh_when_lagged`. Not handled: the search palette, which only adds streamed connector results to an answer it asked for itself; a missed `search.results` shows fewer rows until the next keystroke. The doc comment of `GET /api/v1/events` (part of the OpenAPI text) now names `notification.new`, `notification.delivery`, `integration.call` and `stream.lagged` | reload the page on a lag (loses what the person was doing); replay missed events with `Last-Event-ID` (needs an event log on the server) | remove `LAGGED` from `TOPICS` | docs/reviews/2026-11-12-sec0-review.md SEC0-9; web `events::tests::a_lagging_stream_concerns_every_page` (the topic rule only; no browser test sends a lag)
DEC-655 | sec1 (SEC-1 a, DEC-536): which security headers does the application send, where are they set and with which values? | One middleware (`server::security_headers::layer`), the outermost layer of every route (`Server::guarded`), so refusals of the other layers (cross-site 403, 413, 405) carry them too; a header a handler set itself is kept. Values: `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`; `X-Frame-Options: DENY` (and `frame-ancestors 'none'` in every policy; the first also holds in report-only mode, where a browser ignores `frame-ancestors`); `Referrer-Policy: same-origin` (not the `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` the proxy page named until now: pages have addresses that are secrets, share, invitation and reset links, and another site need not learn even the name of a home instance; requests to the instance keep the full address, and the cross-site check reads `Origin`, which same-origin requests still send); `Permissions-Policy` switching off accelerometer, camera, display-capture, geolocation, gyroscope, magnetometer, microphone, midi, payment, usb (only names browsers know; the app uses none of them); a content security policy on every answer: the page policy of DEC-656 on `text/html`, `default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'` on everything else (JSON, files, pictures: data that loads and runs nothing when a browser is made to show it as a document). The reverse-proxy page now says the proxy sets none of them (a second CSP at the proxy would apply in addition), its Caddy and nginx snippets lost the header lines, and its check asks for each header once | Headers at the proxy only (SEC-1 c, rejected by the owner); `strict-origin-when-cross-origin`; `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy` and `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` as well (not asked for, not tested, left out) | Remove the layer in `Server::guarded` and restore the header table of reverse-proxy.adoc | owner ruling SEC-1 a (DEC-536); security-audit.md SEC-1 (branch wip/fable-reviews); server tests `security_headers::every_answer_carries_the_security_headers` (seen red), 9 unit tests in `security_headers.rs`
DEC-656 | sec1: what is the content security policy of a page, how do the inline scripts get their nonce, and what was verified in a browser? | Policy: `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'nonce-N' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'; style-src 'self' 'nonce-N'; style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: https: [http:]; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self' [end-session origins]; frame-ancestors 'none'`. Nonce: the middleware makes 128 random bits per request and puts them in the request (`CspNonce`); the page context (`Server::web_context`) gives them to Leptos as its `Nonce` in place of the one leptos_axum makes, so the hydration script, the scripts that carry streamed data and (new) the theme script and the brand style of `web::shell` carry the nonce the header of the same answer names; the JSON config block is data and carries none. Verified with the Playwright guard (DEC-660) against deliberately strict policies: (1) without `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` the bundle is refused (`script-src blocked wasm-eval`) and no page hydrates, so it is needed; `'unsafe-eval'` is not, and is never sent. (2) Without `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'` 553 violations in the suite: the server renders `style` attributes (progress and rating widths, cover placeholders from the thumbhash or the dominant colour, brand swatches), and an attribute can carry neither nonce nor a fixed hash; allowed, because a style attribute runs no script and whoever can inject one can inject an `<img>` as well. (3) Pictures: `https:` for any host as v1-plan.md P7/B9 asks (covers straight from TMDB, TheTVDB, IGDB, Open Library with its redirect to archive.org, podcast hosts; no host list to maintain) plus `data:` for the placeholders; `http:` is added only while the public URL itself is plain http (an instance on the LAN, the test suite with its TMDB stand-in; over https a browser upgrades or blocks such pictures before the policy is asked). Connections (server functions, REST, the event stream) and fonts: this origin only. `form-action`: the sign-out form posts to the instance and is redirected to the provider's end-session endpoint. CSP Level 3 runs the `form-action` check of a form navigation again for each redirect; so the policy names the origin of the end-session endpoint of every enabled provider (`auth::oidc::Oidc::end_session_origins`, kept current where the outbound allowlist is). What was seen: the Chromium of Playwright 1.64.1 does NOT refuse that redirect (the `sso` sign-out test passed on a build with `form-action 'self'` alone), so the entry is a precaution for browsers that follow the specification there, not something a test here holds; WebKit, which is said to check redirects, does not start on the build host. Sign-in round trips are links, not forms. Possible limit in such a browser: a provider that redirects on to another host during logout is stopped (the local session is already ended); documented on the reverse-proxy page. Under `cargo leptos watch` only (`LEPTOS_WATCH`), `connect-src` also names the live-reload socket. `server.csp_report_only` (default false) sends the same policy as `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`; there is no report endpoint, the browser console is where the reports are read | Hashes instead of a nonce (the hydration script and the streamed chunks differ per answer); a host list for pictures (breaks with every new source and with podcast artwork); removing the `style` attributes (a nonce'd style element per bar, or widths set by script after hydration); answering the sign-out post with a page that navigates by itself instead of a redirect (no `form-action` entry needed, changes DEC-318) | Change `Headers::page_policy`; set `server.csp_report_only = true` to stop enforcing without a new build | CSP Level 3 (https://www.w3.org/TR/CSP3/: `wasm-unsafe-eval`, `style-src-attr`, the form-action navigation check); leptos 0.8.23 `nonce.rs`, leptos_axum 0.8.10 `provide_contexts`, leptos_integration_utils 0.8.9 (streamed `<script nonce>`), read in the cargo registry; server tests `security_headers::a_page_gets_the_page_policy_and_other_answers_a_closed_one`, `every_inline_script_and_style_carries_the_nonce_of_the_answer` (seen red), `oidc::rp_initiated_logout_is_local_first` (assertion written with the change); sec1-progress.md "Red 1", "Red 2"
DEC-657 | sec1: when is `Strict-Transport-Security` sent? | Only when `server.public_url` starts with `https://`, as `max-age=<server.hsts_max_age_secs>` (new setting, default 31536000), on every answer; `0` sends none. No `includeSubDomains` and no `preload`: the application does not know the other names of the domain. The application speaks plain HTTP behind the proxy and does not look at `X-Forwarded-Proto` for this: the public URL is the operator's statement that the instance is reached over HTTPS. The setting exists because the header cannot be taken back (a browser that saw it refuses plain HTTP for a year) and because a proxy may send its own (a browser reads only the first of two) | Always send it (breaks the LAN test over http); only for requests whose `X-Forwarded-Proto` is https (needs the trusted-proxy rule for one more header, same result for a correct setup); no setting | `server.hsts_max_age_secs = 0` | RFC 6797 7.2 and 8.1 (https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6797); server test `security_headers::hsts_follows_the_public_url` (seen red)
DEC-658 | sec1: which answers get a cache directive? | `Cache-Control: no-store` on every answer without a directive of its own whose content type is `text/html` (pages, the 404 page, the redirect interstitial) or whose path starts with `/api/` or `/_fn/`. Unlike the audit's proposal the public shared-list page is not exempt: for a signed-in visitor its shell carries their name. Unchanged: `/assets` and `/brand/fonts` immutable, handlers with their own directive, files of the site root and `/healthz` without one. Bundle files see DEC-659 | `private, no-store` (the second word adds nothing); `no-cache` on pages (a shared cache or the back button may then show a signed-out person the last page) | `Headers::apply` | security-audit.md SEC-1 (c); RFC 9111 5.2.2.5; server test `security_headers::answers_that_depend_on_the_session_are_not_stored` (seen red)
DEC-659 | sec1, OPS-8 (the part DEC-623 left): how do the bundle files get content hashes in their names? | `hash-files = true` in the cargo-leptos metadata: the build writes `app.<hash>.js`, `.wasm`, `.css` (22 characters of URL-safe base64) and `hash.txt` next to the server executable; `HydrationScripts` and (new, in the shell in place of `<Stylesheet>` in `App`) `HashedStylesheet` read it. A `/pkg` file with such a name answers `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`; any other `/pkg` answer keeps `no-cache` and the conditional `304` of DEC-623 (also a hashed name that does not exist). At start the server uses hashed names whenever the hash file is there, whatever `LEPTOS_HASH_FILES` says, and refuses to start when hashes are asked for and the file is missing (a page would otherwise name files that do not exist and stay without scripts). The Dockerfile copies `target/release/hash.txt` to `/usr/local/bin/hash.txt` and sets `LEPTOS_HASH_FILES=true`; the Playwright configuration sets it too. Not verified here: the image build (no Docker on the build host), in particular that `cargo leptos build --release --frontend-only` followed by `--server-only` leaves `target/release/hash.txt`; if it does not, the image fails at start with the message above | Keep fixed names with `no-cache` (one conditional request per file and page load); a query string with the version (caches treat it less reliably) | `hash-files = false`, `<Stylesheet id="leptos" href="/pkg/app.css"/>` back in `App`, drop the two Dockerfile lines | cargo-leptos 0.3.11 (`hash-files`, README of https://github.com/leptos-rs/cargo-leptos); leptos 0.8.23 `hydration/mod.rs`, leptos_meta 0.8.7 `stylesheet.rs` (read); server test `security_headers::hashed_bundle_files_are_kept_for_good` (seen red), `infra::bundle_tests`; names seen in `target/site/pkg` after a build
DEC-660 | sec1: how does the browser suite catch a policy violation? | `src/web/tests/fixtures.ts` exports the `test` every spec uses. An automatic fixture collects, in every browser context of a test (the built-in one and those opened with `browser.newContext()` or `browser.newPage()`), each `securitypolicyviolation` event (through an init script and a binding; enforced and report-only) and each console message that names the content security policy, and fails the test in its teardown with the list. `global.setup.ts` has a test that fails when a spec takes `test` from the Playwright package. Seen red twice with deliberately strict policies (DEC-656) | `bypassCSP` (hides what the guard is for); a check in a few specs only | Import `test` from `@playwright/test` again | Playwright 1.64.1 docs (fixtures, `BrowserContext.exposeBinding`, `addInitScript`; https://playwright.dev/docs/test-fixtures); sec1-progress.md
DEC-661 | Review of sec1, SEC1-5 (adds to DEC-656): what does `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'` allow someone who can inject markup, and why does the directive stay for now? | DEC-656 named only "a style attribute runs no script". The whole picture: the directive allows declarations on the injected element itself. That is enough for (a) an overlay (`position:fixed; inset:0`) that covers the page with a fake form or link, the phishing case on an internet-facing instance although scripts are blocked; (b) hiding content; (c) a request to any https host through `background:url(...)`, which is no more than an injected `<img>` already gets under `img-src https:`. It does not allow selectors, so no reading of attribute values through CSS (that needs a style sheet; `<style>` needs the nonce and `<link>` this origin), and it runs no script. The risk is accepted for v1 because it needs a markup-injection bug first (Leptos escapes text and attribute values; none is known) and because removing the directive is not a small change: the four sources (`crates/ui/src/progress.rs`, `crates/ui/src/rating.rs`, `crates/web/src/widgets.rs` cover placeholders, `crates/web/src/admin/mod.rs` brand swatches) render on the server and again in the browser, where `style=...` strings and cloned templates go through the attribute, so each needs another technique on both sides and visual re-tests (553 uses in the browser suite). Follow-up package `sec1b` in docs/fable/round2/next-packages.md: rework the four places, then drop the directive | Rework the four components inside the review fixes (not small, see above; the reviewer says the same); a hash per attribute value (`'unsafe-hashes'`; the values are computed widths and colours, not a fixed set) | Nothing to reverse now; after `sec1b`, remove `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'` from `Headers::page_policy` and the test row | docs/reviews/2026-11-13-sec1-review.md SEC1-5; CSP Level 3 `style-src-attr` (https://www.w3.org/TR/CSP3/#directive-style-src-attr); sec1-progress.md "Red 2" (the 553 events)
DEC-662 | Review of sec1, SEC1-4 (corrects one clause of DEC-659 and DEC-623): which directive does a `/pkg` name that does not exist get? | `no-store`, like every page. Such a request is answered with the 404 page, a document with a nonce and, for a signed-in visitor, the shell with their name; DEC-658 says no `text/html` answer is stored, and `pkg_no_cache` broke that by putting `no-cache` on every `/pkg` answer first (the header layer keeps a directive that is there). Now `pkg_no_cache` sets its directive only on an answer that is not a document (`security_headers::is_document`, the same test the header layer uses), so the layer puts `no-store` on the 404 page. Unchanged: `immutable` for a hashed name that exists, `no-cache` and the conditional `304` for any other file under `/pkg`. The clause "also a hashed name that does not exist [keeps `no-cache`]" of DEC-659 and the 404 row of DEC-623 no longer hold | Set the directive only for status 200 and 304 (same effect today; the content type says what the rule is about) | Remove the `is_document` condition in `pkg_no_cache` | docs/reviews/2026-11-13-sec1-review.md SEC1-4; server tests `security_headers::hashed_bundle_files_are_kept_for_good` (three names, signed out and signed in; seen red: `no-cache`), `ops::unhashed_bundle_files_are_revalidated` (its 404 row follows)
DEC-663 | Review of sec1, SEC1-6 (extends DEC-659): the server believed `hash.txt` without looking at the site root. Refuse to start or fall back to the plain names when the files it names are missing? | Both, by what the site root has. At start `infra::bundle_hashes` reads the `js`, `wasm` and `css` lines and looks for `<site root>/<pkg dir>/<output name>.<hash>.<ext>`. All there: hashed names. One missing (or no `js`/`wasm` line) and the site has the plain `<output name>.js`: the pages use the plain names and one warning names the stale hash file (the case "built again with `hash-files = false`, old `hash.txt` left in `target/`", the reversal of DEC-659). One missing and no plain bundle either: the server does not start; the message names the hash file, the missing files and the cause ("the site root and the hash file come from different builds") with what to do. That is the development case of the review: `cargo leptos build --release` wipes `target/site` and writes `target/release/hash.txt`, and `target/debug/server` then reads the older `target/debug/hash.txt`; before, every page named files that were gone and the browser suite failed with timeouts. Falling back there is not possible: the site has neither the old hashed names nor plain ones. Unchanged: no hash file and `LEPTOS_HASH_FILES` off means plain names; no hash file and it on means no start | Always refuse (the reviewer's proposal; turns the harmless reversal case into a start failure); always fall back with a warning (in the development case the pages would name `app.js`, which is not there either: the same silent breakage as before) | `infra::bundle_hashes` | docs/reviews/2026-11-13-sec1-review.md SEC1-6; leptos 0.8.23 `hydration/mod.rs` and leptos_meta 0.8.7 `stylesheet.rs` (how the lines are read: `split_once(':')`, the kinds `js`, `wasm`, `css`); unit tests `infra::bundle_tests::a_hash_file_that_names_missing_files_is_not_followed` (seen red: the old check answered "hashed") and `the_hash_file_decides_and_a_missing_one_is_an_error_when_asked_for`. Tried once with the real executable (`target/debug/server`, `LEPTOS_HASH_FILE_NAME` pointing at a hash file with three other hashes): it ends with status 1 and the message above, naming the three missing files
DEC-664 | Review of sec1, SEC1-7, SEC1-8, SEC1-10 (documentation; adds to DEC-655, DEC-656, DEC-657): what does the reverse-proxy page now say about taking HSTS back, about the proxy's own answers and about `form-action`? | (1) SEC1-7: `server.hsts_max_age_secs = 0` only stops the header; a browser that saw it keeps the rule until its age is over, and forgets it only on `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=0` received over HTTPS (RFC 6797 6.1.1), which the application cannot send. The page says so and gives the way back: setting `0`, the proxy sends `max-age=0` while the name still answers over HTTPS (one line each for Caddy and nginx), until every browser has come by once. No new setting value that sends `max-age=0`: taking a host off HTTPS is a proxy matter, and the proxy has to serve HTTPS for it anyway. (2) SEC1-8: "the proxy sets none of them" stays the rule for everything the application answers; the page now names what that leaves bare (the proxy's own answers, for example a `502` during an upgrade) and shows, as optional, Caddy's default form (`header ?X-Content-Type-Options nosniff`, `?X-Frame-Options DENY`, `?Strict-Transport-Security ...`: written only when the answer has no such field), never for `Content-Security-Policy`; nginx has no such form (`add_header` adds a second header) and stays without. (3) SEC1-10: the sentence "the Chromium the project tests with does not [check redirects]" is gone from the operator page; it now says that a browser may check each redirect of a form (CSP Level 3), that the policy therefore names the origin of the end-session address, and that the entry is needed and stays. The observation (one passing `sso` test on one build with `form-action 'self'` alone, Playwright 1.64.1) remains what DEC-656 calls it: an observation, not a reason to remove the origins | A setting value that sends `max-age=0` (works only while the instance is still reached over HTTPS, so it saves the operator nothing); header lines in the nginx snippet with a `map` on `$upstream_http_...` (not verified here, and easy to get wrong) | Edit docs/modules/ROOT/pages/reverse-proxy.adoc | docs/reviews/2026-11-13-sec1-review.md SEC1-7, SEC1-8, SEC1-10; RFC 6797 6.1.1 and 8.1 (https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6797, read 2026-11-13: "A max-age value of zero ... signals the UA to cease regarding the host as a Known HSTS Host"); Caddy documentation, `header` (https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/header, read 2026-11-13: "Prefix with `?` to set a default value for the field. The field is only written if it doesn't yet exist"; such defaults are applied when the answer is written). Not tried on any proxy; not checked: whether Caddy puts the defaults on the error answer it writes itself when the application is unreachable (the page says so)
DEC-665 | Review of sec1, SEC1-1, SEC1-2, SEC1-3, SEC1-9 (tests; adds to DEC-660): what holds the policy guard of the browser suite now, and what was the one-off failure of `item.spec.ts`? | SEC1-1: the failure ("Route is already handled!") was a race in the spec, not in the application and not caused by the guard: the spec released a held route and called `page.unroute` at once; removing the handler makes Playwright continue the routes it still holds, and the handler's own `route.continue()` then finds the route handled. Both places in `item.spec.ts` now wait for the response before `unroute` (the pattern `heldRefetch` in the same file had). The other five `unroute` calls of the suite were read and left: one already waits, two use `unrouteAll({ behavior: "ignoreErrors" })`, two remove handlers that hold nothing after the outcome was awaited. SEC1-2: `tests/policy-guard.spec.ts` is the standing canary. A route answers a document of its own with `Content-Security-Policy: script-src 'none'` and an inline script (the server is not asked). Three tests assert that both signs arrive (the event and the console line) on the built-in page, in a `browser.newContext()` and on a `browser.newPage()`, then empty the list; two tests empty nothing and are marked `test.fail()`, so the teardown of the guard has to fail them, and a guard that stays quiet shows as "Expected to fail, but passed". The wait in those two cannot fail by itself (a loop, no assertion), so the only failure they can have is the guard's. SEC1-3: the setup test "every spec uses the shared fixtures" now refuses any file under `tests/` except `fixtures.ts` (every script file, sub-directories too) that names a Playwright package as a module in any kind of quotes (`@playwright/test`, `playwright/test`, `playwright`, `playwright-core`; import, re-export, `require`, dynamic import); `helpers.ts` takes `expect`, `request` and its types from `./fixtures`. With no way to the package's `test` except through `fixtures.ts`, a helper cannot hand it on. The test carries its own samples (eleven that must match, three that must not). Stricter than the review asked (it would have allowed type-only and `expect`/`request` imports in helpers): one rule without exceptions is simpler to keep. SEC1-9: two rows in `security_headers::every_answer_carries_the_security_headers` (a `303`, the head of the event stream) and the event stream in `answers_that_depend_on_the_session_are_not_stored` (closed policy; `no-cache`, the directive of its own handler, kept as DEC-658 says) | SEC1-2 with `expect(policyViolations.length).toBeGreaterThan(0)` alone (the review's sketch: proves the collecting, not that the teardown fails a test); SEC1-1 with `unrouteAll({ behavior: "wait" })` (removes every route of the page, also ones a test still needs) | Delete `tests/policy-guard.spec.ts`; restore the one-line pattern in `global.setup.ts` | docs/reviews/2026-11-13-sec1-review.md; playwright-core 1.64.1 `coreBundle.js` (`Route.removeHandler`, `_startHandling`, `_unrouteInternal`: with a behaviour other than "default" the handlers are stopped before the patterns are updated), read in `src/web/node_modules`; mutation runs of the canary in sec1-progress.md "Review fixes" (teardown assertion removed: the two `test.fail()` tests "Expected to fail, but passed"; console pattern changed, event name changed, own contexts not watched: the "sees" tests fail; the `context` override removed: still green, because Playwright's built-in context is opened through the patched `browser.newContext` as well, so the two paths back each other up); setup test seen red with the old pattern on the single-quote sample

DEC-675 | v1-polish P7: recent items of the idle palette get the leading box (v1-status 2.3 "no cover box"). Where do the kind and the cover come from, given that the list lives in the browser's `localStorage`? | `RecentItem` gains `kind` and `cover` (both optional when read), written by the item page from the page's item with the same rule as a result row (`palette::thumb`: smallest stored variant, else the provider's address). No request is made for the idle list. An address that no longer answers (a variant that was replaced) shows the icon (LC-5). Rows stored by an earlier build have neither and show the generic icon until the item is opened again; nothing is migrated (greenfield) | read the recent items from the server when the palette opens (a request per open; the list is per browser by design, WP4-4); icon only, never a cover | drop the two fields and `leading` in `palette::idle_items` | docs/fable/round2/v1-plan.md section 2 P7; DEC-640; tests `palette::tests::the_empty_palette_lists_recent_items_then_the_suggestions`, `shell.spec.ts` "palette: Ctrl+Enter adds ..."
DEC-676 | sec0 "left open": the rules matrix lost the focus on the pressed switch, and the focus did not return to "Edit" after Save or Cancel in a channel's edit form. How is the focus kept without rebuilding the matrix differently? | The switch that was pressed is remembered by a selector (`data-nf-switch="<event>|<channel id>"`). It gets the focus back when the save has ended (the switches are disabled while it runs, which already drops the focus) and once more when the matrix has been built from the answer; after "Use the suggestions" / "Only this one" of the offer the same switch gets it. The focus is only ever put back while it is on no control (`keyboard::focus_is_nowhere`), so a person who moved on keeps their place. The edit form of a channel focuses its "Edit" button (`data-nf-edit`) before it closes, on Save and on Cancel. The matrix is still built anew (a keyed matrix is a larger change) | key the matrix rows and cells so they survive (the right fix when the page is next reworked); `aria-disabled` instead of `disabled` while saving | remove `pressed` and the two effects in `notify_rules_view::Rules`; `close` in `EditChannel` back to `editing.set(false)` | docs/fable/round2/sec0-progress.md "Out of scope, noticed"; WCAG 2.4.3; `notifications.spec.ts` "channels, rules ..." and "a channel is edited in place ..."
DEC-677 | v1-polish P2: which instant starts a year in a zone whose clocks skip or repeat the first midnight of January? | Midnight of 1 January on the zone's clocks; when that midnight exists twice the first of the two, and when the clocks skip it the midnight of 2 January (no zone of the tz database skips the first midnight of a year today; the branch keeps the read from failing). The bounds of the read change, not the statement. The year options of the filter already came from the zone-aware year buckets of the statistics | keep UTC and document it | `activity::year_bounds` back to `chrono::Utc` | docs/fable/round2/v1-plan.md section 2 P2; v1-status G9; DEC-239, DEC-601; test `app/tests/activity.rs::the_year_filter_follows_the_persons_time_zone`
DEC-678 | v1-polish P8: what does "What v1 covers" say about books and board games while Open Library (v1-books, lane B) and Wikidata (v1-wikidata) are not on this branch? | The page describes this branch: four kinds with a source (movie, show, video game, podcast), eight without, the generic file as the way in, and the four limits (export carries no plays, show hours need a runtime, a list named "Owned", years follow the time zone). A source comment in the page marks the lines the packages v1-books, v1-wikidata and v1-manual update. Linked from the nav (second entry) and from the Backup section of operations.adoc | write the page for the planned six kinds now (untrue until the other lanes merge) | delete `v1-scope.adoc`, its nav line and the sentence in operations.adoc | docs/fable/round2/v1-plan.md section 2 P8; v1-status.md section 1, G1 to G3
DEC-679 | Review POL-1 (must fix): on `/stats` (and Settings, Admin, Notifications) nothing a sighted person can see said "you are here" on a desktop: LC-1 gave the account menu entry `aria-current="page"` only. How is the entry marked, and what does the plan's acceptance line say now? | The current entry of the account menu is heavier (`font-semibold`) and tinted with the pair the current section of the top bar uses (`bg-primary-soft`, `text-primary`: the same contrast pair, measured in the browser test at 4.5:1 or more). With the focus on it the entry looks like every focused entry (`bg-accent`, `text-accent-foreground`) and keeps the weight, so focus and "current" stay two different signals. The acceptance line of v1-plan.md section 2 is rewritten: "On `/stats` the top bar highlights nothing (it used to highlight Home); the account menu shows Stats as the current entry; on the phone the tab bar marks You". Owner-visible deviation from the plan as first written ("`/stats` highlights Stats in the top bar"): Stats is not one of the five sections of the top bar and does not become one (LC-1) | weight alone (weak on its own in a menu of short words); a check mark icon before the entry (the theme and language groups of the same menu already use the radio look for "chosen"); a sixth top-bar section (LC-1) | `shell::MENU_ITEM`: remove the five `aria-[current=page]:` classes; restore the old line in v1-plan.md | docs/reviews/2026-11-13-v1-polish-review.md POL-1; LC-1; `shell.spec.ts` "single-key shortcuts ..." (computed weight, tint, contrast, focused look; seen red on the build before the change)
DEC-680 | Review POL-2 (must fix): the German text of the bulk question of LC-3 ("Nochmal als weiteres Mal eintragen?" / "Nochmal als weitere Male eintragen?") said "again" twice and used "Male" as a countable object. What does it say? | One episode: "Diese Folge ist schon gesehen. Ein weiteres Mal eintragen?"; several: "Diese N Folgen sind schon gesehen. Alle ein weiteres Mal eintragen?" (the reviewer's wording; it matches "Einmal gesehen" / "N-mal gesehen" of `item-times-watch` and adds no noun). The English pair of LC-3 ("Log it again as a further view?" / "Log them again as further views?") is unchanged. This supersedes the German half of LC-3; both wordings are named here because the text is owner-visible and the owner tests in German | "Noch einmal eintragen?" for both (drops "further view", which is what tells the question apart from the rewatch one); "Als weitere Sichtung eintragen?" (a new noun the app uses nowhere) | `de.ftl` `episodes-again-ask-views` back to the text of LC-3 | review POL-2; LC-3; DEC-538, DEC-589; test `item_words::tests::the_bulk_question_names_a_rewatch_only_where_one_starts` (seen red on the old text)
DEC-681 | Review POL-3: a recent item of the empty palette kept the provider's address of the day its page was opened (usually right after it was added, before the cover was stored), so every Ctrl+K asked the provider's image host although the cover was stored: against the one rule of DEC-640. How does the list follow the rule without a request per open (LC-6)? | Every read the palette makes anyway corrects the list: the answer of a search and each streamed source answer hand the cover of every item they name (`palette::thumb`, the one rule) to `recent::refresh`, which rewrites the rows of those items in `localStorage` and in the open palette (`recent::refreshed`). The item page still writes the whole row whenever it is opened. A stored cover (`/assets/...`) is never replaced by a provider's address or by none through an answer (an answer may know less than the item page; only the item page writes whatever it has). No request is added. What is left, and written into operations.adoc: an item opened once right after it was added, and not named by a search since, keeps the provider's address until one of the two happens | store only same-origin addresses and show the icon otherwise (review option b: honest in five lines, but an instance with `fetch_policy = lazy` would never show a cover on a recent row, and P7 asked for the box with the cover); one request for the recent items each time the palette opens (LC-6 decided against it); accept and document only | remove `refresh_recent` and its two calls in `palette.rs`, `refresh` and `refreshed` in `recent.rs` | review POL-3; DEC-640; LC-6; tests `recent::tests::a_fresh_read_replaces_the_remembered_cover`, `shell.spec.ts` "palette: Ctrl+Enter adds ..." (a stale address put into the list is replaced after a search; both seen red)
DEC-682 | Review POL-4: the rules matrix put the focus on the pressed switch whenever the focus was "on no control" after the save. In Safari and in Firefox on macOS a click does not focus a button, so the focus was put (and the page scrolled) where it never was; and the switch was forgotten when the defaults offer outlived the view. When is the focus handed back? | Only when the switch had the focus at the moment it was pressed (`keyboard::focus_is_on` in `toggle`): keyboard, and pointer in the browsers that focus a clicked button. The focus is handed back with `preventScroll` (`keyboard::focus_in_place`): a switch that had the focus is where the person left it, and whoever scrolled on while the rule was saved stays there. The selector of the switch is kept inside the `Offer` (`kept("nf-offer")`, which outlives a fresh render of the view), so the answer to the offer hands the focus back whenever the offer itself is still there. Checked while building: a look at another tab does not render the Rules view afresh and leaving the page drops the offer too, so the case the review describes (offer kept, switch forgotten) only arises when the page's own read is repeated after an error; the browser test of the offer passes with and without this part. Still true from LC-7: the focus is only put back while it is on no control, and the matrix is still built anew | track the last input device (pointer or keyboard) instead of asking where the focus is (two more listeners, and wrong for a pointer click in Chromium, where the focus is on the switch and is lost by the rebuild); keyed rows and `aria-disabled` so nothing has to be restored (the right fix when the page is reworked, LC-7) | `notify_rules_view.rs`: `pressed.set_value(Some(selector))` in `toggle`, drop `switch` from `Offer`, `keyboard::focus` in `refocus_soon` | review POL-4; LC-7; WCAG 2.4.3; `notifications.spec.ts` "channels, rules ..." (a dispatched click, which focuses nothing, leaves the focus on the page: seen red; the offer answered after a look at the Channels tab: a guard, green with and without the kept selector)
DEC-683 | Review POL-6: on a podcast the hints of the date choice named a button that is not there ("Mark season, Mark show and Mark up to here ..."). What do they say? | A `-listen` pair like the other labels of LC-2 (`EpisodeWords::range_hint`, `release_hint`; keys `episodes-range-hint-listen`, `episodes-release-hint-listen`): en "Mark season, Mark podcast and Mark up to here spread the episodes evenly from the first to the last day." / "... log each episode on the day it was released."; de "Staffel, Podcast und bis hier markieren verteilen die Folgen gleichmaessig vom ersten bis zum letzten Tag." / "Staffel, Podcast und bis hier markieren tragen jede Folge an dem Tag ein, an dem sie erschienen ist." (with umlaut and sharp s in the file). The hints of every other kind keep the approved text. "as listened" in "Also mark the N earlier episodes as listened?" stays (LC-2; the plan wrote "Mark S1 E1 listened"; the reviewer calls "as listened to" a matter of taste) | reword both hints without button names for every kind (changes an approved text of the show) | `EpisodeWords::of`: the two show keys for the listen verb; delete the two keys per locale | review POL-6; LC-2; test `item_words::tests::the_date_hints_of_a_podcast_name_its_own_buttons` (seen red)
DEC-684 | Review POL-9 (amends LC-8): the comment of `year_bounds` said a skipped midnight takes "the earliest instant of that day" while the code took the midnight of 2 January, and a year for which no bounds can be built silently dropped the filter. What starts a year whose first midnight the clocks skip, and what does an impossible year list? | A day starts at the first instant the zone's clocks show of it (`activity::first_instant`): its midnight; the earlier one when the zone has it twice; when the clocks skip it, the first quarter of an hour after it that exists (01:00 where daylight time starts at midnight, the next midnight where a whole day is left out; at most 25 hours are tried). This replaces "the midnight of 2 January" of LC-8, which would have put all of 1 January into the year before. A year for which no bounds exist (beyond the calendar of chrono, or `i32::MAX`, whose next year does not exist) lists an empty page; the filter is never dropped. No zone of the tz database skips the first midnight of a year anyone logs in, so nothing changes for real data | answer `invalid_query` for an impossible year (the year comes from a link the page built itself; an empty list is the plain answer); keep the fallback of LC-8 and correct only the comment | `activity.rs`: `first_instant` back to the two-branch match, `bounds` back to `q.year.and_then(...)` | review POL-9, POL-10; LC-8; tests `app` `activity::tests::a_day_starts_at_its_first_instant_on_the_zones_clocks` (Sao Paulo 2018-11-04, Havana 2018-11-04, Apia 2011-12-30; seen red), `tests/activity.rs::the_year_filter_follows_the_persons_time_zone` (impossible years; seen red), `the_year_filter_west_of_utc_and_in_southern_daylight_time` (Los Angeles, Sydney, Santiago; a guard, green from the start)
DEC-685 | Review POL-11 (recorded as known, nothing built): a provider cover whose address starts with `http://` (some podcast feeds) on an instance served over https. With DEC-640 such an address is also shown on palette rows and recent rows; the browser does not load it as it is and the CSP of package sec1 allows `http:` pictures only when the instance itself is on http | Known and accepted for v1: the place shows what every cover that does not load shows (LC-5): the title in its box, on a palette row the icon of the kind; each refusal is one line in the browser console. The cover appears once the instance has stored it (the server fetches it, the page then uses `/assets/...`). Written into operations.adoc ("Which hosts a browser contacts for pictures") and into "Limits to know" of v1-scope.adoc. To be written next to DEC-640 at merge | hand the address out as `https:` when the source gave `http:` (in the connector or `api::dto::cover`; breaks hosts without https and belongs to the connectors, not to this package); leave `cover_url` out for `http:` addresses on an https instance (the title box without a failed request; needs the public URL in the DTO layer) | nothing to reverse; a later package picks one of the two alternatives | review POL-11; DEC-640; LC-5; sec1 `security_headers.rs`
DEC-686 | Review POL-12: below 40 rem the key hints of the palette's active row are not drawn (LC-4). At 400 % zoom a desktop of 1280 px is 320 px wide, so a sighted keyboard user who zooms does not see them. Are they reachable? | Yes, checked and pinned by a test, not changed: at 320 px both hints are in the description of the search field ("Enter: Open. Ctrl+Enter: Add as Planned or next step", for assistive technology), and the shortcut help ("?" or the account menu) lists both keys under "Search" as visible text and reflows at 320 px. The row itself stays without hints there (LC-4: they lay over the title). Not a failure of WCAG 1.4.10 (no function is lost) | a line of their own under the title below 40 rem on devices with a fine pointer (`@media (hover: hover)`; the reviewer's proposal: a second layout of the row, for after v1); show them in the palette's footer | nothing to reverse (only assertions were added) | review POL-12; LC-4; `shell.spec.ts` "palette at 390 px ..." (320 px part; a check, green from the start)
DEC-687 | Review POL-5: `sharing.spec.ts:55` ("promote, accept, see history, unlink, merge and undo the merge") ran into the default 30 s in two of three full runs of this lane under the load of the other lanes. Does this lane give it a longer timeout? | No. LANES.md (process rules of 2026-11-13): a test that hits its timeout only under load is reported, not given a longer timeout. The orchestrator judges it in the gate of the main checkout (the test alone and the whole `chromium` project on merged main with no other lane building). The reviewer's reading stands as a note for that gate: about 25 navigations without `test.setTimeout` while its neighbours of that length have 90 to 120 s; nothing this package changed adds a server cost to it. The file is not touched here | `test.setTimeout(90_000)` in this lane (forbidden as a workaround, and sec1 edits every spec) | nothing to reverse | review POL-5; LANES.md "Process rules"; v1-polish-progress.md "Review fixes"
DEC-688 | Review POL-13, POL-14: where is "Which hosts a browser contacts for pictures" written, and under which setting name? | A section of that name in operations.adoc (after "Storage report"): the rule of DEC-640 in one paragraph, the hosts (image.tmdb.org, artworks.thetvdb.com, images.igdb.com, the artwork hosts of podcast feeds), where it happens (search rows, cards and the item page of a new item, recent items), how `storage.fetch_policy` (`library` by default, `eager`, `lazy`) changes it, and the two things to know (recent items, LC-12; `http://` covers, LC-16). The key is `storage.fetch_policy` (configuration.adoc); DEC-640 and the plan wrote `assets.fetch_policy`, which does not exist. "Limits to know" of v1-scope.adoc gains "Covers can come from the sources' hosts" with a link to it; the same page now says that anime tracked as a show counts as a show, and that a line of the import file needs `source_id`, `kind` and `title`. The sentence about the content security policy describes main after sec1 (this branch still documents `frame-ancestors` only) | a page of its own (three paragraphs do not carry one); the privacy text of the instance (there is none in the docs yet) | delete the section and the entry of v1-scope.adoc | review POL-13, POL-14; DEC-640 "Consequences written down"; docs/modules/ROOT/pages/storage-budget.adoc "Fetch priority"
DEC-689 | Review POL-7, POL-8, POL-16: which of the small notes are built, which tests change, what is left? | Built: `command::Leading` shows a picture again when it loads after a failure (`on:load`, as `widgets::Cover` does); `shell::account_entry` matches Notifications by prefix (`/settings/notifications/...`). Tests: a browser test holds the page's code back until the cover has failed, so only the check at mount can find it ("a cover that failed before the page's code ran ...", seen red with that check switched off); the wasm test of the key hints that only restated its class string is deleted (the browser test at 390 px proves the behaviour); the palette test picks a provider cover that is there and one that is not from the rows instead of looking at the first row inside an `if` (every second poster of the fixture is served); the cover wasm test and the podcast page test of the server were each seen red with the code they prove switched off. Not built, one line for a later package: the palette suggestion "Log a play of <title>" and the tab "Plays" of a video game still say "play" although the toast says "Session" (review POL-16.4: outside P3 as written; one wording decision for the owner). Noted for v1-manual: `naturalWidth == 0` also holds for an SVG without an intrinsic size, so a member-typed SVG address would be shown as failed by the check at mount | build the "session" wording of the suggestion and the tab here (more owner-visible text than the review asked for) | remove `on:load` in `command::Leading`; `account_entry` back to the equality | review POL-7, POL-8, POL-16; LC-5; tests `ui/tests/wasm.rs::a_command_row_whose_picture_fails_keeps_the_icon_and_shows_no_text` (extended, seen red), `shell::tests::five_sections_and_an_admin_entry_only_for_admins` (extended, seen red)
DEC-690 | Orchestrator, 2026-11-13 (screenshots of the app, website lane): in the dark theme the radio buttons of the play dialog that are not chosen ("Won" column, "No winner") were solid white discs. How are radio buttons drawn? | By the page, not by the browser, with one class for every native radio button of the app (`ui::RADIO`: the play dialog, the share choice of a list, the hosting choice of the setup wizard and of Admin > Instance, the sign-up and sign-in modes of the admin area): `appearance-none`, not chosen an empty ring (transparent inside, a 1 px edge in the colour of field borders, `--input`), chosen a 5 px ring in the primary colour around a dot of what is behind it, so chosen and not chosen differ by shape and not by colour alone; focus is the ring every control has. Both edges are measured in the browser test at 3:1 or more against the surface behind them, in light and dark (WCAG 1.4.11). They stay `<input type="radio">`: keyboard, grouping and the accessible name are the browser's. Before, `accent-primary` only tinted the browser's own drawing, whose empty state does not follow the theme of the page. Noticed, not built: the native checkboxes (`ui::Checkbox`, Library select, sharing) are still drawn by the browser with `accent-primary` and may show the same on the dark theme | `color-scheme: dark` on the dark theme so the browser draws its dark controls (changes every native control and the scroll bars at once: its own package and owner look); `accent-color` plus a background (no effect on a native radio) | the seven `class=ui::cn(&[..., ui::RADIO])` back to `accent-primary`; delete `ui::RADIO` | orchestrator message of 2026-11-13; WCAG 1.4.11, 1.4.1; `item.spec.ts` "the winner radios of the play sheet are rings ..." (seen red: `appearance` was `auto`)
DEC-691 | Orchestrator, 2026-11-13 (screenshots): a board game play reads "Session logged" in Home "Recent activity", on History and on the History tab of the item. What does the row say? | "Play logged" / "Partie eingetragen" for a board game (new key `history-play`, chosen by `history::session_key` from the kind of the row, for a session row and for the history event of a session, whose progress is `session`: that is the row a play of one's own log shows); every other kind with sessions keeps "Session logged" / "Sitzung eingetragen" (`history-session`, ot2a). The same split as the dialog and its toast (OT-16; LC-2: "Logged: Play" / "Eingetragen: Partie" for a board game, "Session" / "Sitzung" otherwise) and as the Plays tab and the rates of ot2f, which say "plays" / "Partien" (DEC-602 to DEC-604). The line below it (winner, scores, duration, DEC-603) is unchanged. Not changed: `progress-session` ("Session logged" as the label of a progress value of type session in `widgets::progress_label`, still used by the offline queue and the log sheet), which has no kind at hand; the activity row no longer takes its words from there | "Played" / "Gespielt" (reads like the status word, LC-2); one neutral word for both kinds | `session_key`: return `history-session`; delete `history-play` in both locales | orchestrator message of 2026-11-13; OT-16; LC-2; DEC-564, DEC-602 to DEC-604; tests `history::tests::a_board_game_row_says_play_logged` (seen red twice: the first fix covered the session row only, and the browser test stayed red because a play of one's own log is the event row), `item.spec.ts` "a play shows its people, scores, winner ..." (item History tab, History, Home; seen red)

DEC-711 | v1-plan.md 9 left three Open Library details to confirm when the fixtures are recorded: the `key:` query of `search.json`, a query for several keys at once, and the type of a work's `description`. What did the live API answer, and what is built on it? | Checked on 2026-11-12 with ten requests (one per 1.3 s, generic client name, no contact): `q=key:/works/OL82563W` answers that one work; `q=key:(/works/A OR /works/B OR /works/C)` with `limit=50` answers the works the index holds and leaves out a key it does not know, without an error; `description` is a plain string in `/works/OL45804W.json` and `{"type": "/type/text", "value": ...}` in `/works/OL82563W.json`. Built: details are the work record plus one `key:` search for authors, year, median page count and cover (the plan's fallback, up to three author requests per book, is not needed); `OpenLibrary::works` asks up to 50 works with one search; `description` is read in both forms. Every field of a search row but `key` is optional. Ids are checked (`OL`, digits, `W` or `M`) before they go into a path. An item asked by an edition id keeps that id (the work id is not added, so two ids of one namespace never sit on one item and an id another item holds is never claimed by a refresh) | author requests as the plan's fallback (more requests for the same data); add the work id to an item that was imported with an edition id (can collide with an item that already holds the work) | `crates/connectors/src/openlibrary.rs` | https://openlibrary.org/dev/docs/api/search , https://openlibrary.org/dev/docs/api/books (read 2026-11-10); the recorded answers in `crates/connectors/fixtures/openlibrary/`; docs/connectors/openlibrary.md "Verified live (2026-11-12)"
DEC-712 | The plan says to skip `cli match-books` when the owner has fewer than about ten `google_books` rows. The owner's export holds 6 (and 4 `openlibrary` rows, all work ids). Build it? | Yes: the orchestrator's brief for this lane says the title-only stubs "must get matched (dry run first)". Without `--apply` the command writes nothing and prints, per book, the match or the reason there is none. A work is taken only when exactly one of five results has the same normalised title (lower case, punctuation as spaces, a leading article of English, German, French or Spanish dropped), the year agrees or the book has none, no other item holds that work and no other book of the same run matched it. With `--apply` the book gets the id and what the search row holds (title, authors, year, cover, median page count) and stays due for its full refresh; the earlier id stays. A search that fails is that row's reason, not an error of the run | skip it (the owner adds the six books again or sets a page total by hand); match on the first result (guesses); ask Google Books for the ISBN of each id (a second source with its own terms) | remove `Cmd::MatchBooks` in `apps/cli/src/main.rs` and `match_books` in `apps/server/src/book_stubs.rs` | docs/fable/round2/v1-plan.md 3.1.4; the count in docs/fable/round2/v1-books-progress.md; test `book_stubs::tests::match_books_takes_one_exact_hit_and_nothing_else`
DEC-713 | v1-plan.md 3.1.4 and 7 (B1) say a Ryot book stub "is now in state `failed`" and is never refreshed again, and specify `cli refresh-failed` for items in state `failed`. Is that the state of an import stub? | No. The importer creates a stub with `App::find_or_create_catalog_item`, which stores state `partial`, and `App::mark_catalog_refresh_failed` turns only `stub` into `failed`. An import stub whose refresh failed stays `partial` with a retry time and is asked again only when somebody opens its page. `failed` is reached by an item in state `stub` (one whose provider data was purged by the retention job) whose refetch failed. So the command takes both: state `failed`, or never fetched (`fetched_at` empty) and in somebody's library (`CatalogRepo::unfetched_items`). Search results nobody added are also `partial` and never fetched; they are left alone, or the command would ask a source for every row a palette search ever stored. An item needs an id of a registered source that is not switched off; the others are counted in the output. A failed item becomes `stub` again, a `partial` one stays `partial`; both are due at once and get a job on the background lane. The name `refresh-failed` stays (the plan, the brief and the acceptance text use it); the usage text and the runbook say what it covers | only state `failed` as written (would do nothing for the owner's books); every never-fetched item (hundreds of leftover search rows) | `crates/store-pg/src/repos/catalog.rs` `unfetched_items`: drop the second half of the condition | `crates/app/src/catalog.rs` (`put_catalog_item`, `mark_catalog_refresh_failed`), `crates/importers/src/runner.rs` (`stub`); test `books::refresh_failed_requeues_only_items_a_registered_source_knows`
DEC-714 | Operator contact (v1-plan.md 3.1.1, B4): what is a valid contact, where is it kept and how does it reach the requests? | (1) Valid: an e-mail address (`name@host.tld`) or an `https://` URL with a host, at most 200 characters, printable ASCII without spaces and without parentheses (the value is sent inside a comment in parentheses in a header; a line break, a tab, a non-ASCII character or a parenthesis is refused: `catalog_core::check_contact`, API code `invalid_contact`). An internationalised address has to be written in its ASCII form. (2) Kept as the instance setting `instance.operator_contact`: configuration key and `APP__INSTANCE__OPERATOR_CONTACT`, the short form `KK_OPERATOR_CONTACT` named by the plan (the long form wins), else the column `instance_settings.operator_contact` (the settings are a typed row, so migration 0100; the plan expected none). Set by env or file it is read-only in the admin area like every managed setting; a configured value that is not valid stops the start with the reason. Not gated by a fresh proof (it is no credential). (3) One shared `OperatorContact` handle is read by the governed client per request, by the source registry and by the admin API, so a change applies at once in that process. A connector declares `ConnectorInfo::contact` (`None`, `Preferred`, `Required`); the client sends `<ident>/<version> (<contact>)` as the `User-Agent` only for connectors that are not `None` and only when a contact is set; the others keep the brand's header (which may carry the brand's support URL, never the contact). (4) `Required` without a contact: the registry reports the new `CredentialState::NeedsContact`, so search and refresh skip the source, and the client refuses the request before anything is sent (`HttpError::Blocked`). Admins see the state word `needs_contact` ("Operator contact missing", red dot, with Set up in the palette footer); members see `not_set_up` with Ask admin, as for a missing key. No connector is `Required` yet: Wikidata (v1-wikidata) is the first. (5) Open Library is `Preferred`. (6) A separate worker process reads the setting when it starts, like stored source keys; with web and worker in one process (the default) there is nothing to do | a free text field (header injection has to be excluded somewhere; the two forms are what the sources ask for); a key and value settings table (a second mechanism beside the typed row); put the contact into the brand's `support_url` (a brand file is not an instance setting and it would go to every source); `needs_contact` for members too (they cannot set it) | drop the column and the `SettingDef`; `ContactUse::None` on a connector switches it off for that source | docs/fable/round2/v1-plan.md 3.1.1 and 7 B4; docs/connectors/openlibrary.md (User-Agent rule); tests `governed::a_connector_that_wants_the_contact_sends_it_in_the_user_agent`, `other_connectors_never_send_the_contact`, `a_required_contact_that_is_missing_blocks_the_call_without_a_request`, `credentials::a_source_that_requires_the_contact_is_skipped_until_one_is_set`, `contact::tests::a_contact_with_a_line_break_is_refused`

DEC-716 | The page total on the item page (3.1.3): when is it an estimate, who may change it, and how? | (1) "of N pages (estimate)" when the provenance of `attributes.page_count` is `openlibrary` (the median over the editions of a work); "of N pages" when a person set it or an import brought it. The text "of N" became "of N pages" (en) / "von N Seiten" (de). (2) Who: `ItemPageDto::can_edit`, true for everybody on a home instance and for admins only on a hosted one (the item is shared); the route `PUT /items/{id}/page-count` and the server function `set_page_count` check the same rule and answer `forbidden`. v1-manual's "Edit details" can use the same flag. (3) How: "Change" next to the total (accessible name "Change the page total") opens a one-field form in the action bar, not a dialog; Save stores 1 to 100000 with provenance `user`, says "Page total set to N." in a toast and gives the focus back to the button; Cancel closes it. The typed text is kept with the page, so a refetch while the form is open does not lose it (as DEC-477 for the page field). A book without a total shows "Set page total" instead (the six title-only books of the owner need exactly that until v1-manual). (4) Only kinds with the paged progress model whose attributes allow `page_count` take a total (book, comic); manga counts chapters | a dialog (one number does not need one); admins only everywhere (in a household the reader holds the book); no way to set a missing total before v1-manual | `crates/web/src/log_sheet.rs` `PagesInline` without `total`; the route in `crates/api/src/routes.rs` | docs/fable/round2/v1-plan.md 3.1.3; tests `log_form::tests::only_a_total_from_an_estimating_source_is_an_estimate`, `a_page_total_is_a_number_from_1_to_100000`, app `books::a_page_count_is_a_positive_number_on_a_paged_kind`, `books.spec.ts`
DEC-717 | "Finished" on a book with a known total (3.1.3, G16): the button already logged the total and set Completed, as two requests started together. What changed? | The order: the last page is logged first and the entry is completed when that log went through (`next_step::run_step_then`), so the status change never overtakes the page it should count, and a failed page log does not complete the entry. DEC-718 | How do the two shell commands reach Open Library, and what does the batch store? | `server::book_stubs::Catalog::open` connects like the escape hatches (the server's configuration and database, pending migrations applied), registers the same connectors as the server (configuration switches and the admin's source switches are honoured; keys are not read, they are not needed here) and builds its own governed client: the connector's policy (one request per second, the allowlist), no HTTP cache, the User-Agent with the operator contact from the configuration or, if it is not set there, from the stored setting. DEC-719 | ISBN search: which form is sent, and what is the result? | A query is an ISBN when, without hyphens and spaces, it is 13 digits starting with 978 or 979 with a valid EAN check digit, or 10 characters with a valid ISBN-10 check (an `X` allowed last). `/isbn/` is asked with the compact form that was typed (whether the service finds an edition by the other form is not verified), the result is the edition's work as the single row, with the external id `("isbn", "<isbn13>")` and the attribute `isbn13`. An ISBN nobody holds, or an edition without a work, is "no result", not an error; a number with a wrong check digit is searched as text. If the search index does not hold the work yet, the row has the edition's title and nothing else | always ask with the ISBN-13 (one cache entry per book, but unverified for records that list only an ISBN-10) | `by_isbn` in `crates/connectors/src/openlibrary.rs` | docs/fable/round2/v1-plan.md 3.1.2; tests `openlibrary::an_isbn_is_recognised_by_its_check_digit`, `an_isbn_query_asks_the_isbn_endpoint_and_returns_its_work`
