DEC-723 | Orchestrator, 2026-11-13 (a defect seen on screenshots): Home says "0 books finished" although three books are Completed. "Completions" in the statistics and goals counted only events that moved an entry to Completed (DEC-594, OT2E-3, deferred there "for the stats package"); a book never has such an event (`finished` is refused for a paged kind, "Finished" and an import set the status), nor has a show or film set to Completed by hand. What is a completion? | An entry that reached Completed, as the person sees it: (1) every effective event that moved an entry to Completed counts as before (a film marked watched, the last episode of a rewatch run; a stop marker does not, DEC-590); (2) an entry that is Completed while none of its effective events moved it there counts once, dated by the wall clock of its status stamp (the day the status was set: by hand, by "Finished" on a book, by an import, which is the import day); (3) it stops counting when the status is moved away from Completed, and it never adds a "logged step" (`events`). Not chosen: writing an event when a status is set by hand. Built in the three places that sum the statistics, so totals, series, the year comparison and goals agree: the Rust rules (`app::month_amounts`, `app::window_amounts` take the user's library entries; `stats::history`), the full statement (`month_amounts_full`), the per-item cache (`insert_item_months`) and the goal window (`pg_window_amounts`, whose "touched" items now include entries completed in the window): each adds one row per such entry to its `live` set. Migration `0101_hand_completions.sql`: the SQL function `hlc_time(text)` (the stamp's wall clock) and a row trigger on `library_entries` that marks the (user, item) pair in `stats_dirty` when an entry becomes, stops being or is deleted as Completed, so the cache follows a status set by hand. A cached month is now dropped when it has neither events nor completions (it was: no events), because a month can hold nothing but a completion set by hand. Limits, on purpose: an entry counts at most once by hand, so a show completed by hand and later finished again in a rewatch run counts 1 (the run's event), as before; a status that an undo restored to Completed counts by rule (2) with the day of the undo when no completing event is left; books imported as Completed count on the import day (v1-plan.md Q-C is about their pages, which still count nothing: a completion is not pages) | write a marker event whenever a status is set to Completed (an import would put one row per title into History on the import day, moving the status away would need an undo of the marker, bulk undo and offline sync would have to carry it, and a new progress variant touches every match over `Progress`); count Completed entries only in the Home tile (the tile, the Stats series and a goal "finish 12 books" would disagree) | drop migration 0101, the `UNION ALL` branch of the three statements and `history` in `crates/app/src/stats.rs` (back to `contributions` alone); the cache delete back to `events = 0` | the orchestrator's message of 2026-11-13; DEC-594 (OT2E-3), DEC-601; tests `app` `stats::a_status_set_to_completed_by_hand_is_a_completion`, `store-pg` `phase1::completions_set_by_hand_count_on_postgres_like_the_rust_rules` (both seen red first), `stats_race::updates_of_events_sessions_and_kinds_mark_the_cache` (found the cached month that was dropped), `rewatch::a_stop_marker_ends_the_run_for_good` (expectation 0 -> 1: the show of that test was completed by hand)
DEC-724 | Review BOOK-1 and BOOK-3 (2026-11-13): LB-13 dated a completion set by hand by the wall clock of the entry's status stamp and counted it only while the status was Completed. Every later write of Completed (a second import, a bulk change, the status select, a removal that is undone) moved it to the day of that write, the start of a rewatch run took it out of the history, and a stop put it back on the day of the stop. Where does the completion live, and who keeps it? | On the entry: `LibraryEntry::completed` (`HandCompletion::At(day)` or `Undated`, LB-15), stored as `library_entries.completed_at` and `completed_undated` (migration 0101, rewritten). It is "the completion no event made" and it is independent of the status stamp. Rules, in one place (`LibraryEntry::set_status_by_hand`, used by `App::set_status`, the bulk status change and `App::add_to_library` with a status): a status a person or an import writes to Completed makes one when the entry has none and none of its effective events completed it (an event that moved the entry to Completed is its completion and is counted as before; a stop marker completes nothing, DEC-590); a day once given never moves, whoever writes Completed again and however often; any other status written by hand takes the completion away. A status an event moves (`log_progress`, `undo_progress`) leaves the completion alone: the start of a rewatch run on a show completed by hand keeps it although the status is In progress, a stop gives Completed back with it, and a run watched to the end adds the completion of its finishing event beside it (2, as for a show whose first run has an event). The undo of a bulk status change puts the completion back with the status (`StatusBack::completed`), and a removed entry that Undo restores gets it back (`EntrySnapshot::completed`); both travel in the signed undo token. The statistics read the entry: the Rust rules (`app::stats::history`) and the three statements (`month_amounts_full`, `insert_item_months`, `pg_window_amounts`) add one row per entry with a completion, on `completed_at`; the `NOT EXISTS (completing event)` test is gone from the statements because the rule is applied once, when the status is written. Deviation from the review's and the orchestrator's wording ("kept by the trigger when the status first becomes Completed, cleared when it leaves"): the application writes the two columns, because only it knows whether a person, an import or an event moved the status (a trigger cannot tell the start of a rewatch run from "In progress" chosen by hand, nor an event's Completed from a person's). The database keeps the invariant that matters: the BEFORE UPDATE row trigger `library_entry_completion` keeps `completed_at` of a row that is and stays Completed, whatever the UPDATE says (tested with a plain UPDATE behind the application's back). The AFTER trigger `stats_entry_changed` marks the statistics cache when the completion changes and no longer for a status write that changes nothing about it. `hlc_time()` is gone. Limits: Completed chosen by hand while a rewatch run of a show that an event completed is running adds nothing (the event is its completion); an entry whose completing event is undone and whose status stays Completed counts nowhere until Completed is written again | a trigger that sets and clears the column from the status alone (cannot keep the completion through a rewatch run, and would give every film that an event completed a second completion); keep deriving from `status_hlc` and never rewrite the stamp for an equal value (breaks last-writer-wins for the status; an undo and a restore still write new stamps); a marker event per status write (LB-13 says why not) | drop the two columns, the index `library_entries_completed`, the triggers and `LibraryEntry::completed`; the statements back to LB-13's branch | docs/reviews/2026-11-13-v1-books-review.md BOOK-1, BOOK-3; DEC-587, DEC-590, DEC-594; tests (all seen red first) app `completions::completed_written_again_keeps_its_day`, `a_bulk_completed_over_completed_entries_keeps_their_day`, `a_removed_and_restored_entry_keeps_its_completion`, `rewatch::a_rewatch_keeps_the_first_completion_where_it_was`, importers `generic::importing_twice_keeps_every_completion_in_its_month`, store-pg `phase1::a_completion_by_hand_keeps_its_day_on_postgres`; domain unit tests `library::tests::*` (written with the code)
DEC-725 | Review BOOK-2, orchestrator's default of 2026-11-13 (the owner may reverse it): LB-13 counted every entry that an import or a person set to Completed on the day the status was written, so an import of years of history put every finished show and book into the month of the import, into this year's side of the year comparison and into a goal of this year. On which day is such a completion? | On the newest step that is logged for the entry at the moment the status is written (the newest effective event's `occurred_at`): an imported show lies on its last imported episode, an imported book on its last imported page log, "Finished" on a book on the page it has just logged (today). An entry without any logged step is "undated": it counts in the all-time totals (Home and the Stats tiles, the book count) and in no month, no year, no year comparison and no goal. The same for every kind (shows, films, books). An undated completion gets its day when Completed is written again once a step exists (the importer writes the status before and after the history, so an imported title with history is dated by its history in one run); a day once given stays (LB-14), also when later steps are logged. In the data an undated completion is the month `""` of `MonthAmounts` (`app::UNDATED`): totals sum it, `stats_series`, goals, the goal window and the year comparison leave it out. Said in one line where a person meets it: under the result of an import ("A title the file calls Completed is finished on the day of its newest history entry. Without any it has no date and counts in the totals only, not in a month, a year or a goal.") and as a footnote under the Stats and Home tiles when the person has such completions (`StatsTotalsDto::undated_completions`), en and de. Owner-visible consequence to weigh: a person who sets a book or a show to Completed by hand without ever logging a step gets an undated completion; it does not count towards "finish 12 books in 2026". "Finished" on a book with a known total always logs the last page first and is therefore dated today | the day of the status write for a completion without events (LB-13: wrong for imports, right for "I finished it today" without a log); the day of the status write only when the write is not an import (the importer would have to say so through `set_status`, and a status select on an old title would still claim today); the entry's `added_at` (the day of the import again) | in `HandCompletion::from_history` answer `At(<time of the write>)` instead of `Undated` (and pass that time in from `App::hand_completion`), or do so only when the caller is not the importer; the two strings `stats-undated-note` and `imports-completed-note` go with it | the orchestrator's brief of 2026-11-13 for this review; docs/reviews/2026-11-13-v1-books-review.md BOOK-2 (a); DEC-594, DEC-601; tests app `completions::a_completion_by_hand_lies_on_the_newest_logged_step`, `a_completed_entry_without_a_logged_step_is_undated` (seen red), importers `importing_twice_keeps_every_completion_in_its_month`, web `time_tiles::tests::an_empty_or_small_total_...` (the footnote)
DEC-726 | Review BOOK-4: migration 0101 left every statistics cache that was built before it without the completions set by hand (the trigger only hears later writes), so Home and Stats kept saying "0 books finished" on the databases of the acceptance run while a goal counted them. | The migration ends with `UPDATE stats_users SET zone = ''`: every user's next read rebuilds in full (the mechanism a changed time zone uses). DEC-759 | What does the progressive delay of DEC-535 count, so that nobody can keep an account shut by guessing? | The count belongs to the pair of account name and client address (`crates/auth/src/throttle.rs`): the first `auth.throttle.free_failures` (3) failures of a pair cost nothing, the next one a wait of `delay_base_secs` (1 s), doubling with each further failure up to `delay_max_secs` (300 s); during a wait the pair is answered `429` with `Retry-After` and nothing is verified; a success clears the pair; a pair without a failure for `window_minutes` (15) starts again. Guesses at `admin` from one address therefore never make the owner wait at another address (the test "the admin name cannot be locked by guesses from another address"). A wait per account alone would be a lock in effect: whoever guesses again the moment a wait ends keeps the account waiting. Known and accepted: guesses at one account spread over many addresses are bounded per address only (pair delay and address budget), not per account; what bounds them is the password length rule (10 characters), TOTP where enrolled (whose wrong codes are counted per account alone, LA-12), and the verification cap. Unknown names are counted like known ones, so the wait reveals nothing | a wait per account across all addresses (a lock for the owner under a steady attack); a hold inside the request instead of a refusal (ties up connections, needs a queue per account) | key the pair without the address in `Throttle::begin` | DEC-535; security-audit.md SEC-5; next-packages.md sec2 tests
DEC-760 | The budget of a client address | `auth.throttle.address_failures` (30) failures across all names per window of `window_minutes` (15), a fixed window that starts with the first failure; over budget, every check from the address is refused (`429`, `Retry-After` until the window ends) for every name. Attempts being checked count against the budget, so a burst of parallel requests cannot run ahead of its own failures or fill the queue of the verification cap. A success does not refill the budget (one known account would otherwise pay for guesses at others). IPv6 addresses are counted per /64 network, IPv4 per address. The address is the one `server.trusted_proxies` yields (DEC-284); callers without an address (the command line) are slowed per account only. Behind a proxy that is not listed in `server.trusted_proxies` every visitor has the proxy's address and shares one budget: the reverse-proxy page says so, and the server logs one warning when a request with `X-Forwarded-For` arrives from a peer that is not trusted | no budget in flight (a burst of thousands of parallel guesses would all be verified); a sliding window (more state per address for little gain) | `Throttle::begin` | DEC-535; security-audit.md SEC-5 scenarios b and c
DEC-761 | Where the throttle applies | Every check of a secret: sign-in and the local sign-in (password, TOTP, recovery code), "Is this your account?" with a password, re-authentication, the current password of a password change, the code that confirms an authenticator, reset links (no account is known: the address budget only) and emailed sign-in links (pair of the ticket's account and the address). One count per pair for all of them. The client address reaches `auth` through a task-local set by the request layer (`auth::client::scope` in `api::request_meta::layer`), not through a parameter of each of the roughly sixty call sites | an address parameter on every checking function | pass the address explicitly and drop `auth::client` | security-audit.md SEC-5; task brief

DEC-763 | The cap on concurrent password verifications | `auth.throttle.verify_concurrency` (8) argon2 verifications at once per process, a semaphore around every verification (passwords and recovery codes); the next ones wait their turn. DEC-769 | Request read timeouts (deferral of DEC-653) | Three settings in seconds. `server.header_read_timeout_secs` (150): hyper's HTTP/1 header read timeout; `axum::serve` never ran it because it sets no timer, so the server has its own accept loop (`apps/server/src/serve.rs`, hyper-util, same graceful shutdown). hyper starts that timeout whenever it waits for a request head, so it is also the keep-alive idle time; the default is therefore above the time Caddy (2 minutes) and nginx (60 seconds) keep idle upstream connections by default, or the proxy could send a request into a closing connection. `server.body_idle_timeout_secs` (30): longest pause between two pieces of any request body. `server.body_read_timeout_secs` (60): a whole body, for every request except the import upload `POST /api/v1/imports` (64 MiB). A late body is answered `408` `request_timeout`. The limits run while something reads the body. With them, the pages router collects a body only for `/_fn/`; other paths get it unread behind a length limit (they read none), so a stalled body there pins nothing. HTTP/2 connections have no head timeout (a proxy speaks HTTP/1.1 to the application) | a head timeout of 30 s as the first draft had (below both proxies' defaults: sporadic resets on reused connections); `tower_http::timeout::RequestBodyTimeoutLayer` (idle limit only, a new direct dependency) | `axum::serve` back in `main.rs`; drop `read_timeout::layer` from `Server::guarded` | DEC-653; hyper 1.12 `server::conn::http1::Builder::header_read_timeout`; https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/reverse_proxy and https://nginx.org/en/docs/http/ngx_http_upstream_module.html (read 2026-11-13)
DEC-770 | Review SEC2-1: wrong one-time codes for one account spread over many addresses | A third count per account alone (key `t|<username>`), added on every wrong second factor (TOTP code or recovery code) at sign-in, the local sign-in, re-authentication and authenticator enrolment, with the pair's growing wait (`free_failures` free, then `delay_base_secs` doubling up to `delay_max_secs`, a quiet `window_minutes` starts it again); during the wait the code is not checked (`429`, `Retry-After`), a right code clears the count. It is checked only after the right password, so a stranger cannot make the owner wait at the second step (test "wrong passwords do not touch the code count"); someone who has the password can make the owner wait there, which is accepted (the password is known and has to be changed anyway). With the defaults this allows about 13 codes per 24 minutes (4 free, 9 growing waits, then a quiet window), about 800 a day, against 10^6 codes of which three are right at a time: more than a year on average. No notification to the admin after N such failures yet (out of scope, listed) | the review's suggestion to check the count in `Throttle::begin` whenever a code is sent (refuses before the password is checked; same bound, but a stranger who sends any code could then see that the account is waiting) | drop `code_begin`/`code_settle` in `Auth::second_factor` and `totp_confirm` | docs/reviews/2026-11-13-sec2-review.md SEC2-1; DEC-535

DEC-772 | Review SEC2-14: three small throttle points | (a) `free_failures` near `u32::MAX` no longer overflows (no wait at all then). The item page's lists cell has no boundary of its own and reads `lists` under the page's `Transition`: it sits inside the keyed render of the item, so a boundary there is a new one on every refetch and showed its fallback (empty) while `lists` was read again (seen in the test); the page's Transition is the same one across refetches and keeps the last lists. On the first load the page now waits for `lists` as well. The other `<Suspense>` places of the diagnosis' list were read and left alone, their resources are never refetched: `history.rs` group switcher and filter fields (`kinds`, `companions`), Stats (`home.rs`, `totals` and `kinds`), `stats_extras.rs` (`stats_compare`), `bulk.rs` (the list picker reads `lists` once per opening). The price (review RACE-8): the first load of the item page, on the server and after a client-side navigation, shows the item when `item_page`, `kinds` and `lists` are all there; a `lists` call that hangs keeps the skeleton (and the stream) for the whole page, an error leaves the cell out as before. Accepted: `lists` is one indexed read per user and the cell is above the fold; if it ever gets slow, read it into a signal by an effect in the browser instead. | Mirror signals filled by effects (more state for the same result) | `Suspense` again | race-diagnosis.md 4 F6, section 5

DEC-844 | Fencing of job claims (F-2, CI green; `jobs` lease test failed on GitLab with "claimed again while still running") | Without a migration the claim itself is the fencing token: every claim increments `attempts` (returned in `Job`), and `extend`, `complete`, `fail` and `release` only touch the row `WHERE id = $1 AND state = 'running' AND attempts = <the claimed attempts>`; each returns whether it still held the job. The worker logs a warning when the heartbeat finds the job lost and when its result is discarded; the handler is not interrupted, so jobs stay at-least-once (a job runs twice in that rare case, but a stale worker no longer overwrites the newer claim's state, e.g. re-queues or kills a job another worker runs). Tests: `a_stale_claim_cannot_extend_complete_or_fail`, `a_stale_last_attempt_does_not_mark_the_job_dead`; `a_job_longer_than_its_lease_runs_once` now uses a 3 s lease and a 4 s job (renewal every 1 s, so it has real slack) instead of 300 ms / 1.5 s. DEC-853 | Undo of a view "watched with" left its session behind (#1, OT-22) | The link between a view and its session is the derivation that already existed: the session id comes from the event's client op id (`app::view_session_id`), so no column and no migration. Undo deletes the session when owner and item match. DEC-860 | Notifications sent twice by two runs (#6, OT-37 part 1) | A run claims before it sends, in one statement: `NotifyStore::claim_delivery` turns `queued` into the new state `sending` with the claim time in `sent_at`, `claim_digest` does so for the channel's `digest` rows due by `until`; a run that claims nothing sends nothing.
